    SkySaga reverse-engineering reference

    Generated by docs/reference/build.py from the repository. Client addresses are SkySagaU.exe build 36731 (image base 0x400000, no ASLR). Claims in articles are marked [code] (read from disassembly), [run] (observed live), [guess].

    RPC coverage

    Direction: C2S client→server, S2C server→client, from which client stub calls the RPC stats hook FUN_00BB3E50(dir, ordinal, bits, json). Status is what the emulator does with it.

    Directionhandledimplementedpartialunhandledmissingunknowntotal
    C2S30125155
    S2C176176
    both1910
    ?0

    Client-to-server RPC payloads (36731) docs/protocol/client-rpcs.md

    Payloads of the RPCs the client sends, read from their send stubs. Each stub calls FUN_00BC56B0(ordinal, ...) to start the packet, writes the fields inline or through a writer, and then optionally logs the call as JSON. Most JSON printers are in the packed part of the binary, so field names are only known where a printer could be read. The stub addresses are in docs/reference/data/rpc_client.json.

    Conventions: "ranged max M" is 32 - clz32(M) bits. "Full-width u32" is 32 bits in network (big-endian) byte order. Server handlers live in Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets.

    Ordinal RPC Stub Payload
    18 UISettingsSetHotbarSlotItemSpec FUN_009C3BE0 slot ranged max 5 (3 bits), hand ranged max 2 (2 bits, 0 = left, 1 = right), item reference (writer FUN_0085A490)
    19 UISettingsSelectHotbarSlot FUN_009C30B0 slot ranged max 5 (3 bits)
    17 UISettingsSetGameplayCameraState FUN_009C37D0 third-person camera 1 bit, state ranged max 1 (1 bit, unknown)
    20 UISettingsSetMiniMapSettings FUN_009C4080 2 bools, then two lists (FUN_009C4C90: count escaped at 0x19; FUN_009C2F80). Not handled yet
    21 UISettingsSetUISettings FUN_009C4420 see below, 8 bits
    22 UISettingsSetControlSettings FUN_009C3460 1 bit (meaning unknown)
    51 SetPlayerState FUN_00B25D10 state ranged max 10 (4 bits)
    76 SetInputMovement FUN_00B24140 direction 3 × ranged 0x80 of (v + 0x40), speed ranged 0x78 (see entity-sync.md)
    135 JoinDestinationSelectionWorld FUN_007B85E0 triggeringEntityID: full-width u32
    155 EntityMoved FUN_00B2A460 X 17, Y 15 (+0x3FC0), Z 17, yaw 15 bits (see entity-sync.md)
    160 SetLookAtDirection FUN_0086D540 pitch, yaw: each ranged max 0x6400 of (v + 0x3200), 15 bits, 1/32° (FUN_00859000)
    170 TouchedVoxelTrackingSyncUp FUN_00B2D5C0 count escaped at 100 (7 bits), then per entry a full-width u32 and 8 bits (FUN_00B2DB60). Not handled yet
    280 PlayerDodged FUN_00B25580 ranged max 8 (4 bits)
    295 PerformJump FUN_00B23E30 nothing

    UISettingsSetUISettings (21)

    Writer FUN_009C4DE0, JSON printer FUN_009C4AF0 (field names below):

    field wire
    DisableAutoQuestTracking 1 bit
    DisableSocialNotifications 1 bit
    DisableMysteryBoxNotifications 1 bit
    DisplayTimeMode ranged max 2 (2 bits)
    QuestTrackerSort ranged max 4 (3 bits)

    Observed bytes 00 and 08. 08 is DisplayTimeMode = 1.

    Settings come back through sync

    The RPCUISettings* calls only report a change. The client applies settings from the Player's clientsettingscomponent (bindings vtable 0x013456C0, read FUN_00C265A0), so the server stores them there and the binding syncs them back. They are also saved in player.json under Settings.

    binding sync parameter wire
    0 74 hotbarslotresources count escaped at 6 (3 bits), then per slot a full-width u32 (the server sends the slot number) and two item references, left hand then right hand (FUN_00C26E70 / FUN_00C26D30). Verified: items show on the hotbar
    1 94 lastactivatedslot ranged max 5 (3 bits, FUN_00C25D30)
    2 33 currentgameplaycamerastate third-person camera 1 bit (+0xBD; 0 = first person, the client default, so the player never sees their own character), then state ranged max 1 (+0xBC, FUN_00C25C50)
    3 116 minimapsettings not sent yet
    4 161 uisettings the same 8 bits as RPC 21 (FUN_00C27030)
    5 27 controlsettings 1 bit (+0xBE)

    SetPlayerState (51)

    The value is the local character state byte (character state object +0x28), sent from FUN_00B24BB0 whenever it changes. Values seen: 0, 2, 3, 4, 5. When the state is 7, the same function also sends PlayerDodged. If entity table entry 0x5B exists, it also sends the RPC from FUN_00B251C0. The state names have not been found. The Player's own characterstateid (sync 22) is the server-side counterpart, not yet synced.

    Equipped items

    clientequippeditemcomponent (bindings vtable 0x01341B1C, read FUN_00BFF590): equippeditemspecs (sync 50) is a count escaped at 9 (4 bits), then one item reference (FUN_007E9440) per equipment slot. The slots are LeftHand, RightHand, Head, Torso, Legs, Arms, Device, Backpack, AdditionalHeadFeature (names at 0x013415D4). autoequip (sync 9) is 1 bit, and legactions (sync 102) is a presence bit + u32. With armour equipped and the camera in third person, the customised character shows in the world (verified 2026-10-12).

    Entity sync format (36731) docs/protocol/entity-sync.md

    How entity state travels from server to client: the EntityAdd / EntitySync packets, the per-entity sync block inside them, and the parameter encodings of the world-level entities (TimeOfDay, WorldComposition). All addresses are SkySagaU.exe 36731 (no ASLR).

    BitStream conventions

    RakNet BitStream: +0 bits used, +8 read offset, +0xC data pointer.

    • ReadBits(dst, n, alignRight=true) fills dst little-endian per byte. A field of n bits written by the server as WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(v), n, true) reads back as v.
    • Ranged ints are written in 32 - clz32(max) bits (Util.NumBitsRequiredUInt32 returns the leading-zero count, despite its name).
    • Ranged indices into a client table of count entries use 16 - clz16(count - 1) bits (WriteRangedIndex). Index 0 means "unset"; real entries start at 1.
    • BitStream.Write(BitStream) consumes the source's read pointer. Serialising the same SyncData twice without ResetReadPointer() sends an empty block the second time (this is why only 13-byte EntityAdds reached the client before the fix).

    Escaped length (FUN_007D7490)

    Used both for the outer length in EntityAdd/EntitySync and for the inner parameter-block length:

    bits meaning
    1 escape: 0 = short form, 1 = long form
    18 length in bits (short form, <= 0x20000)
    32 length in bits (long form)

    EntityAdd (client stub FUN_007F1140)

    field encoding
    type hash optional (presence bit + 32 bits)
    entity id int
    parent id optional
    sync block length escaped length
    sync block see below

    The stub copies the sync block into its own stream and wraps it (FUN_00C71550). The RPC dispatcher (FUN_00DBAE80 / FUN_00DBAE00) builds the packet stream from packet+0x30, length at packet+0x28.

    Sync block (Entity_ApplySyncEx FUN_00B43760)

    1. Dirty mask: one bit per synced parameter (SyncedParametersCount, e.g. Player = 169). - Full 32-bit words: written byte-reversed (big-endian). The client reads each word and swaps it (FUN_00B44AD0). - Tail (count % 32 bits): natural order, right-aligned. It is swapped only on a big-endian host (FUN_00EA76A0), so never on x86. Reversing the tail drops parameters 160..168 of Player.
    2. Parameter block length: escaped length (above). Leaving out the escape bit shifts every parameter by one bit. This was the cause of the "flaky islocal" bug.
    3. Parameters: for each set mask bit, in sync-index order, the parameter's own encoding.

    Client side lookup

    • The entity type's param table sits at type+0x44..0x48, 0x1C bytes per entry: +8 component hash, +0x10 binding index, +0x18 sync index. type+0x86 = param count.
    • Components sit at entity+0x90. Each component's bindings object is at component+0x20, and vtable slot 2 is read(binding, stream).
    • Sync index order is the alphabetical order of parameter names in Data/Entities.json. Binding order is the component's own order. They differ, so always map through the names.

    World entities

    TimeOfDay — clienttimeofdaycomponent

    Read FUN_00D462E0, bindings vtable 0x0135DCC8.

    sync binding parameter wire stored
    0 2 daynightcycleduration ranged, max 0x780 ×1/64 → +0x14
    1 1 fixedtimeofday bool +0x20 bit 0
    2 3 realworldstarttime 64-bit +0x08
    3 0 starttimeofday ranged, max 0x10000 +0x10
    4 5 timeofdayoffset ranged, max 0x10000 +0x1C
    5 4 timestretch ranged, max 0x1FC0 ×1/64 → +0x18

    Timer_GetFraction (FUN_00D45E60) divides by daynightcycleduration, so a zero duration crashes the client. Known issue: the server writes the two halves of realworldstarttime in the wrong order.

    WorldComposition

    Client components: clientadventurequestscomponent (primaryquestdata, secondaryquestlist), clientworldcompositioncomponent (palettecrc, terraintype, minwalkableyposition), eventmanagercomponent (activeeventlist, eventcalendar).

    Sync indices (Entities.json): activeeventlist 0, eventcalendar 1, minwalkableyposition 2, palettecrc 3, primaryquestdata 4, secondaryquestlist 5, terraintype 6.

    clientworldcompositioncomponent: factory FUN_00B07110 → ctor FUN_00B35020 (vtable 0x01333AA4) → base FUN_00C7A0F0 (vtable 0x013498F8, bindings sub-object at +0x38, vtable 0x013498D8). Binding names → indices: FUN_00B15040. Read function: FUN_00C7A7E0.

    binding parameter reader wire stored
    0 palettecrc FUN_007D7C90 ranged index into the BiomePalette table, 6 bits for 46 entries +4 (u16)
    1 terraintype FUN_00BD82A0 3 bits (max 6) +6 (u8)
    2 minwalkableyposition FUN_00C7A610 8 bits = value + 100, clamped to -100..100 +8 (i32)

    Each change sets a dirty bit at this-0x30 (1, 2, 4) and notifies the owner through this-0x38 vtable +0xC.

    palettecrc is an index, not a CRC. The table is the client's loaded BiomePalette list:

    • [0x01487BCC] + 0x19DC: pointer vector. Entry 0 is null ("unset"), entries 1..45 are palette objects (0x380 bytes each, CRC of the name at +0xC).
    • +0x19E0: count = 46.
    • After the pointers: the CRCs sorted, a u16 permutation (sorted → index), and the CRCs in index order. This is how the client resolves a name CRC to an index.
    • The name CRC is the engine's lower-case CRC32 (bhash).

    Indices measured in a live client:

    index palette CRC
    29 BiomePalette_FTUE_The_Old_City 0xBC9FADBF
    43 BiomePalette_SocialHub 0x00B42798

    These match MapSpec slot 4 (also 1-based, count 46).

    TerrainType enum (name table at 0x01404A0C, followed by Noof):

    value name
    0 Sky
    1 Sky_BonusWorld
    2 Surface
    3 Peak
    4 Subterranean
    5 Enclosed

    GeoData uses these names in DefaultTerrainType / Terrain. FTUE_The_Old_City is Sky.

    SeaLevel (not yet implemented server-side)

    Components: clientnodecomponent (nodecrc, disableworldnode, inifiniteupdatedistance, deletenodeatdistance) and sealevelcomponent (seafloorlevel, sealevel, showsea). Sync: nodecrc 0, seafloorlevel 1, sealevel 2, showsea 3 (default true). The readers have not been traced yet.

    Player parameters checked against the client readers

    One wrong width shifts every later parameter of the entity, and nothing on the client reports it: values silently become garbage. SKYSAGA_SYNC_TRACE=1 logs every parameter the server writes (sync index, component, name, width) so each one can be compared with the reader below. A component's TrySync must return false for every parameter it does not write. Returning true sets the mask bit without data.

    Fixed 2026-10-12. Together these were why the player could not move and the HUD never unlocked:

    sync parameter client reader wire
    12, 36 availableplayersigillist, currentsigil ClientPlayerNameComponent were not sent at all (TrySync returned true without data). Now: list = count escaped at 0x100 (9 bits), per entry sigil index (5 bits) + 2 full-width u32 (FUN_00C535D0 / FUN_00C53760); currentsigil = ranged index into the 27-entry sigil table (5 bits, FUN_00C53190)
    28 craftingdropslots FUN_00D18620 count escaped at 2 (2 bits), then exactly 2 item refs
    78 inputmovementdirection FUN_00BF46F0 case 2 / FUN_007D6590 3 × ranged 0x80 of (v + 0x40), ×1/64
    79 inputmovementspeed case 3 / FUN_00BF4580 ranged 0x78, ×1/8
    81 inventoryentitylist FUN_00BC7550 count escaped at 0xF9 (8 bits), full-width u32 ids
    114 maxinventoryslots FUN_00C3F490 ranged 0xF0 = 8 bits (was 6)
    135 position FUN_00BF88F0 / FUN_007D66A0 X 17, Y 15 of (y·64 + 0x3FC0), Z 17 (was 17/17/17)
    162 unlockedfeaturecrcset FUN_00C426B0 count escaped at 62, then presence bit + full-width CRC32 of the feature name, sorted ascending (the client binary-searches the set; see client/ui-screens.md, HUD visibility)
    — scale FUN_007D6410 ranged 0x38, ×1/8 (was raw float bits)

    Checked and correct: the aspect bools (CanEditMap 0 … IsSpectator 5, IsDebugPlayer 9, IsLocal 0x11: 1 bit each, FUN_00C75410), takeonly (1 bit), joblist (count escaped at 64), locationspecificquestsready (1 bit), playername (string), yawdegrees (15 bits), the customisation flag and CRCs.

    Item reference (FUN_007E9440, InventoryItemRef)

    field wire
    kind ranged index into the item table ([0x01487BCC]+0x1F28, count +0x1F2C = 1810) → 11 bits
    amounts count escaped at 4 (3 bits), each presence + u32
    quantity presence + full-width u32
    item uuid presence + 4 × u32
    detail 2-bit kind; 0 → optional uuid, 1 → string, 2 → nothing

    Inventory item slot (inventoryitemcomponent.inventoryslotdata)

    inventorydefinition::ItemSlot, field names from the reflection registration FUN_00E02D10, binary reader FUN_00C08300:

    field wire
    ResourceNameCRC ranged index into the item table (11 bits, FUN_007D8110), not a CRC
    Amount 1 bit "large", then 32 bits if set, else ranged max 0x40 (7 bits) (FUN_00C07960)
    IsUnlimited 1 bit
    LosableAmount as Amount
    CraftRandomSeed ranged max 0x10000 (17 bits, FUN_00C078D0)
    UUID presence bit + 4 full-width u32 (FUN_007EA3B0)

    The item table index is GeoData Resources Index + 1: the client keeps the 1809 resources in order behind a null entry 0 (live check: entry +0x8 = name CRC, +0xC = index). The server reads the order from Data/Resources.txt (ItemTable). Sending the 32-bit name CRC here was the FTL index assert at 0x00BA8306 that SKYSAGA_NO_INVENTORY used to dodge (fixed 2026-10-12). An unknown name maps to index 0 and shows as an empty placeholder in the inventory.

    Movement

    The owning client sends RPCSetInputMovement (76): direction 3 × 8 bits (v + 0x40) and speed 7 bits. It moves only after the server has applied the input to clientcharacterphysicscomponent and synced it back. RPCEntityMoved (155) reports the resulting position: X 17, Y 15 (+0x3FC0), Z 17, yaw 15 bits, with no entity id. Verified 2026-10-12: holding W moved the character from z = 3.5 to 26.9 in 3 s.

    Live table sizes ([0x01487BCC] + offset, 36731)

    offset table count
    0x19C8 backstories 22
    0x19E0 biome palettes 46
    0x1DDC materials 522
    0x1EC0 sigils 27
    0x1F2C items 1810
    0x201C tribes 36
    0x3E70 material customisations 266

    UUIDs on the wire

    Four 32-bit words, behind a presence bit. The client prints each word low nibble first, so word i = int(reverse(hex group i of ToString("N")), 16), written as big-endian bytes (BitStreamExtensions.WriteClientUuid).

    RPC ordinals (client 36731) docs/protocol/rpc-36731.md

    Read from the client's RPC name table at 0x01409524 (342 entries). Wire ordinal = table index - 1; entry 0 is the string RPC-CALL, not an RPC. These match Servers/SkySaga.Game/PacketId.cs.

    Character creation and the new-player flow (36731) docs/client/character-creation.md

    How a new character is meant to reach the creator, from GeoData.json and the client code.

    Intended flow

    1. FTUE_The_Old_City (Adventures[78], wire 79, IsFTUE: true): the scripted tutorial. It has an intro video, cutscenes, and movement/attack unlocked by cutscene OnCompleteActions.
    2. DestinationSelection (Adventures[64], wire 65, WorldType DestinationSelection, RootFeature CharacterSelect, palette BiomePalette_Forest_Traditonal, ToD MidDay_Fixed, MapSize HomeIsland_6x4x6): the airship. The character is created here.
    3. Home island / social hub. Airship_Leave_To_CoFL (AnimatedSequences[2]) ends with TeleportToWorld { DestinationType: SocialHub }.

    From the airship to the creator

    DestinationSelection.AdventureStartedClientActions
  -> DestinationSelectionIntro            Actions[27], ConditionalAction on HasQuestFromSource
       (false) -> Trigger_CharacterCreateIntro   Actions[160], TriggerAnimSequence
                  CC_EquipHelmet
  -> Character_Create_Intro               AnimatedSequences[4], prop PilotHelmet_CC
       OnCompleteActions: Action_ShowCharacterCustomisation, UnequipHelmet
  -> Action_ShowCharacterCustomisation    Actions[3], RunMeandrosAction (ExportedActions[3])
  -> faGame_ShowCharacterCustomisation    script function FUN_01207B60

    FUN_01207B60 gets the local player and its ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent. It switches to game state 0xE (GameState_CharacterCreation) when IsCharacterCustomised is 0 (component +0x68 bit 0) and the useusername option is off.

    Then come Character_Create_Complete (AnimatedSequences[3], prop PaperMap_CC) and Character_Create_Post_Loop (5).

    Game and frontend states

    The game state machine is the object at ModeGlobal + 0xCE0; its state pointers start at slot 0x338. The switch is FUN_00718D00(id), and the current id is at ModeGlobal + 0xD34.

    id state
    0x9 GameState_Gameplay
    0xD GameState_ReadyToPlay
    0xE GameState_CharacterCreation (ctor FUN_00810290, enter FUN_00810410, vtable 0x012C2D38)

    The frontend machine is at ModeGlobal + 0xD38, with 12 states switched by FUN_00718B90(id):

    id state
    3 DownloadCharacterList
    5 CharacterCreation (not reached in 36731)
    8 SelectCharacter
    0xB JoinHomeworld

    DownloadCharacterList always goes to SelectCharacter, and SelectCharacter always goes to JoinHomeworld. In this build the frontend never creates a character; creation happens in game.

    GameState_CharacterCreation enter (FUN_00810410) opens UI screens 0x42 (ui_portalfade), 0x6B (ui_animatedPrompt_v3) and 0x35 (ui_accountSetup_Back).

    Saving

    1. The client checks the name: /api/account/name/checkName. The reply is read by FUN_00810C00 for the keys valid, profane, containsNotAllowedCharacters, alreadyExists and suggestion.
    2. If the name is valid, it sends RPCSaveCharacterName (162, C2S, stub FUN_008113B0) and waits.
    3. The server answers RPCCharacterCreationResponse (163, S2C, reader FUN_007EDE70, one ranged field, max 4). FUN_007150E0 forwards it to FUN_00810710 only while the game state is 0xE. Result 0 continues; result 1 logs "Character Saved Unsuccessful".

    The appearance is sent as RPCSetCharacterCustomisationData (53, C2S, stub FUN_00B21A10): a 32-bit value, then the struct written by FUN_00B22A00.

    clientcharactercustomisationcomponent (Player)

    Factory FUN_00A8EAA0 (0x78 bytes) → ctor FUN_00B21620 (vtable 0x01331248) → base FUN_00BF9620 (vtable 0x01340F10). The bindings sub-object is at +0x20 (vtable 0x01340EF0); its read function is FUN_00BF9D60. Name → index: FUN_00A9E970.

    binding name reader wire
    0 CustomisationData FUN_00BFA830 struct, see below
    1 CustomisationListCRC FUN_007EA4B0 optional u32 (presence bit + 32 bits)
    2 ScaleRangeCRC FUN_007EA4B0 optional u32
    3 IsCharacterCustomised bit bool, +0x48 bit 0 of the bindings object
    4 InitialSignatureColour FUN_00BF9AB0 u16 (not in Entities.json)

    Player sync indices: customisationdata 37, customisationlistcrc 38, ischaractercustomised 83 (scalerangecrc is also a binding).

    CustomisationData

    Field names from the client's own RPC log. Wire order is the order FUN_00BFA830 reads in:

    field reader wire example
    Gender ranged, max 2 2 bits 0 male, 1 female
    Tribe FUN_00BF9B30 ranged index (u16) Human, Cat, Lizard, Monkey
    MaterialList FUN_00BFA000 list of material indices ["Human_Skin_Pink1","Human_Eyes_Dark_Brown","Fabric_Yellow"] (skin, eyes, clothes)
    AttachmentList FUN_00BFA4C0 list of {Attachment, Material} 5 slots: hairstyle, ears/extra, beard, …; "" = empty
    MaterialCustomisationList FUN_00BFA260 list 9 slots: markings ×3, pattern palette, 3 empty, eye, mouth
    Backstory FUN_00BF9A30 ranged index (u16) ""

    Example (logged by the client):

    {"entityID":9,"customisationData":{"Gender":0,"Tribe":"Human",
 "MaterialList":["Human_Skin_Pink1","Human_Eyes_Dark_Brown","Fabric_Yellow"],
 "AttachmentList":[{"Attachment":"HumanMaleHairstyle00_Bald","Material":"Human_Hair_Brown"},
  {"Attachment":"","Material":"Human_Hair_Brown"},
  {"Attachment":"HumanMaleBeard01_None","Material":"Human_Hair_Brown"},
  {"Attachment":"","Material":"Human_Hair_Brown"},{"Attachment":"","Material":"Human_Hair_Brown"}],
 "MaterialCustomisationList":["Pattern_HumanMaleMarkings00_None","Pattern_HumanMaleMarkings00_None",
  "Pattern_HumanMaleMarkings00_None","PatternPalette_Human_01","","","",
  "Eye_Human_Male02_Cute","Mouth_Human_Male02_Cute"],"Backstory":""}}

    The creator sends this on every change (tribe, gender, colour), not only at the end. The RPC payload is 264 bits: the 32-bit entity id, then this struct.

    Server status

    • SKYSAGA_ADVENTURE=ftue|airship|home selects MapSpec.Ftue / DestinationSelection / HomeIsland.
    • The intro video is sent only for the FTUE.
    • The web stub answers checkName with valid: true (at the root and inside result).
    • SetCharacterCustomisationData: the 231-bit struct is kept on the connection.
    • SaveCharacterName (a string, read after the escaped ordinal): the server sets Playername, CustomisationData and IsCharacterCustomised = 1, replies CharacterCreationResponse(0) and saves. Verified 2026-10-12: the client leaves the creator and stays in Gameplay. Before IsCharacterCustomised was synced it looped Gameplay ↔ CharacterCreation.
    • ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent syncs the raw struct, absent CRCs and the flag. All of it is persisted in the player save (IsCharacterCustomised, CustomisationData as base64). Synced by default since 2026-10-12. The earlier crash (FUN_00C18D00) was the shifted Player stream, not the struct. With the third-person camera and equipped armour, the created character (MiNo) shows in the world with its hair and colours.
    • RPCJoinDestinationSelectionWorld (135, C2S, {"triggeringEntityID":5}, sent when the player uses the airship map) is parsed and logged, not acted on yet.

    Loading screen / "white screen" (client 36731) docs/client/loading-screen.md

    All addresses are SkySagaU.exe 36731 (image base 0x400000, Ghidra program /36731). Status markers: [code] read out of the disassembly, [run] observed on a live client, [guess] not yet confirmed.

    ui_loading_screen gate (first suspect, see below: not the final cause)

    ui_loading_screen, tick = FUN_008F6EE0 (vtable 0x012E8BFC, slot 5). [code]

    tick(this):
    if this[0x194] == 0:
        switch this[0x17C]:          // loading-screen state machine
            case 0: photo slots ...  // ownerName / displayName text boxes
            case 1: ...
            case 3: tips (/_suggest), waits FUN_007abbe0()
            case 4: if !this[0x160] && ModeGlobal[0x1044] && FUN_007abbe0():
                        start background/teleport visuals; this[0x160] = 1
    else if MapSpecValid(ModeGlobal + 0xFC4):        // FUN_00C0CD80
        FUN_008F8780(ModeGlobal + 0xFC4)             // fills map-related list at +0x1B0..+0x1C0
        if list non-empty: this[0x17C] = 4
        this[0x194] = 0

    +0x194 is a request flag. While it is set the state machine does not run, and the panel stays on screen over the world and over fullscreen video. It looked like the white screen, but see "The white screen was ui_portalfade".

    MapSpecValid (FUN_00C0CD80): spec[0x2E] != 0 && spec[0x30] != 0 && spec[0x0C] != 3. [code] In practice these are Searchable[0], Searchable[2] and a type byte. A default-constructed spec (FUN_00C0CC30, 0x60 bytes) fails the check.

    ModeGlobal + 0xFC4 (current MapSpec)

    ModeGlobal is a singleton at DAT_0143785C, ctor FUN_00713050, dtor FUN_00713F70.

    Every instruction touching +0xFC4, classified [code]:

    Address Function Role
    0x00713124 FUN_00713050 ctor default-construct
    0x0071473A FUN_00713F70 dtor destroy
    0x00715E2E FUN_00715E10 reset to an empty spec, then game state -> 0xC / 0xB (callback keyed by DAT_0144A058, registered in ctor; also referenced by FUN_008BD3C0)
    0x0080FB21 FUN_0080F9E0 read (GameState_UnloadWorld enter)
    0x008F6F2B FUN_008F6EE0 read (loading-screen tick, above)
    0x00964270 FUN_00964010 read (gameplay tick; copies spec OUT into a temp)
    0x00964BB0 FUN_00964BA0 the only writer: TeleportBegins handler
    0x00BAC266 FUN_00BAC260 getter return p + 0xFC4

    FUN_007176F0 is MapSpec::operator= (this = destination, arg = source).

    TeleportBegins (RPC ordinal 35 / 0x23)

    • Registered by FUN_00964C00(FUN_00964BA0, state). The caller is FUN_009644F0, the gameplay state's Enter, which also clears flag bits 1 and 2 and sets bit 0 (flags = flags & 0xF9 | 1). The handler does not exist before gameplay is entered, so a 35 sent earlier gets dropped. [code]
    • Receive stub FUN_00964EE0 reads exactly one MapSpec (FUN_007E9810) and calls the handler. [code]
    • Handler FUN_00964BA0: state.flags(+0x38) |= 2; ModeGlobal.FC4 = spec. [code]

    Gameplay tick after TeleportBegins (FUN_00964010)

    state.flags at +0x38: bit0 = entered, bit1 = teleport pending, bit2 = ready. [code]

    if !(flags & 2):
    if DAT_01440E98[0x70]: "Level unload"; flags |= 5
else:
    "Level unload"; flags.bit2 = <world/scene ready checks: FUN_007D08A0, FUN_00B43D70, FUN_006EF5F0,
                                  FUN_00785770(0x42), FUN_008DC8D0, FUN_00965280, FUN_007ABBE0>
if (flags & 5) == 5:
    FUN_00C62DF0()
    if !DAT_01440E98[0x70] && FUN_006EF5F0() && !IsScreenOpen(0x45):
        OpenScreen(0x45, arg = copy of ModeGlobal.FC4)   // ui_loading_screen, FUN_00777A40
    ModeGlobal.state.Change(0xC)                 // FUN_00718D00, states 0..0xE at ModeGlobal+0xCE0

    0x45 is the UI screen id of ui_loading_screen (its ctor FUN_008F6660 registers it). FUN_00777A00 / FUN_00777A40 are the screen manager's IsOpen / Open, see ui-screens.md. [code]

    The white screen was ui_portalfade, not the loading screen

    Live snapshot of the white frame [run]: ui_loading_screen (0x45) was closed, its tick at fsm 4. The open overlay was ui_portalfade (screen 0x42, ctor FUN_008DC580), stuck on its Fade animation. The intro video was playing underneath, faintly visible through the white.

    ui_portalfade [code]

    Animation/event name hashes are in globals: 0x01456EEC PortalFadeLeaveBiome, 0x01456EF0 PortalFadeEnterBiome, 0x01456EF4 Fade, 0x01456EF8 CancelFade.

    Function Effect
    FUN_008DC780(this, leave) leave=1: +0x139 = 1, plays LeaveBiome/Fade (fade to white). leave=0: +0x138 = 1, plays EnterBiome/Fade (fade out of white)
    FUN_008DC810 if +0x139: clear it, play LeaveBiome/CancelFade
    FUN_008DC8D0 "leave fade finished", one of the teleport readiness checks in FUN_00964010

    Opened (id 0x42) by the Enter functions of two game states, FUN_0080E730 and FUN_00810410, right before they call the gameplay Enter.

    Who drives it: the local player entity [code]

    The player entity class (vtable 0x012D2344, ctor FUN_0086B7F0) has request fields +0x91 (fade requested), +0x92 (leave flag), +0x93 (cancel requested) and timestamp +0x88/+0x8C.

    • Tick FUN_0086BD40: if +0x91 and the time is due, FUN_008DC780(+0x92). If +0x93, FUN_008DC810.
    • Entity events FUN_0086B9C0(id): 0x23 requests a fade, 0xA4 requests a leave-fade (+0x91/+0x92 = 1/1), 0x57 requests a cancel.
    • Parameters changed FUN_0086B8A0(list): binding 17 (islocal) fires event 0xB and, when local, sets +0x91 = 1, +0x92 = 0 (EnterBiome, fade out of white).
    • All three return immediately unless +0x81 & 0x08.

    +0x80 low 12 bits hold the bool params of clientplayeraspectscomponent in binding order (bit 5 = isspectator ... bit 11 = islocal). The value 0xFFFFF000 was read live. +0x50 = teleportingmethod.

    The server never sent islocal, so the bit stayed clear, so nothing ever faded the white out.

    Fix (server)

    1. TeleportBegins is off (EnteredGameplay.SendTeleport = false). No reconnect anymore. Without it ui_loading_screen (0x45) closes by itself [run], so the +0xFC4 gate above does not matter for the white screen.

    2. Live proof [run]: writing +0x81 |= 8 and +0x91 = 1 with WriteProcessMemory lifted the white at once.

    3. PlayerAspectsComponent.IsLocal + Entity.GetSyncData(forOwner, onlyParameter): the owner alone gets islocal = true.
    4. Five EntitySync copies (islocal only), 2/4/6/8/10 s after EnteredGameplay, sent from PlayerConnection.Tick on the server thread. Sending from a Task.Delay thread raced ProcessMaps over SyncingForOwner and sent false. [run] Whether a single copy at 2 s lands varies from run to run. The first accepted copy lifts the fade, and the later ones are ignored. Open: about 4 s after accepting it, the client clears islocal by itself. EntityMoved packets stop at that moment, and resending true does not set it again. The white does not come back, but local-player control is lost. [run] A copy sent in the same tick as EnteredGameplay was ignored; the delayed one applied (flags 0xF0 -> 0xF8, portalfade +0x138 = 1, frame average 250 -> ~20). The delay is a stand-in until the real trigger is found.
    5. Not in the initial EntityAdd: there it crashed the client in READY_TO_PLAY with STATUS_FLOAT_MULTIPLE_TRAPS at 0x00D45EDC (zero duration into FUN_00D45E60 via FUN_00BE0540). That crash is still open; it means some local-player timer data the server should send is missing.

    Dead ends, kept so nobody repeats them

    • FUN_00715E10 does not wipe +0xFC4 during the reconnect: the spec stayed VALID across it. [run]
    • FUN_008F7B20 (FadeOff/FadeOn/ResetToDefault) is the loading-screen photo slideshow, not its close.
    • The 0x45 that FUN_00964010 uses was misread at first as a value slot. It is a UI screen id.

    UI screens (client 36731) docs/client/ui-screens.md

    The screen manager is [ModeGlobal + 0x103C] (object 0xEEC bytes, ctor FUN_00781DA0). - Screen instances: mgr + 0x18 + id*4, ids 0..0x7C. - Open-screen bitmask: mgr + 0xECC + (id >> 3), bit id & 7. - FUN_00777A00(id): is open. FUN_00777A40(id, 0, arg, ..) -> FUN_00785820: open with an argument (vtable +0x44 receives it). FUN_00785770(id): instance if open, else 0. - Every screen derives from the base widget (ctor FUN_009A45E0(parent, &nameHash64, id, ...)). Its vtable slot 3 is OnOpen, slot 4 OnClose, slot 5 Tick, slot 17 SetOpenArgs. - Names are a 64-bit lowercase CRC (FUN_006E4BA0, table 0x012A2668). The 32-bit event/animation hash is FUN_006E4CA0 (MSB-first CRC32, table 0x012A2268, init 0, lowercased).

    HUD visibility

    Being open is not enough for a HUD screen to show anything. ui_hud_v3a (0x02), ui_xpBar_v3, ui_minimap_v3, the quest tracker and the rest stay empty until two things hold:

    1. ScreenManager +0xEE5 ("HUD shown") is set. FUN_00792310 recomputes it as ScreenManager+0xD98 != 0 && !(ScreenManager+0xEBC & 0x80). +0xEBC is the OR of every open screen's flag word (widget+0x114, set with FUN_009A4920(bit, on) and merged by FUN_00790D70); bit 4 is cursor mode, and bit 7 is set by full-screen menus such as the pause menu. When +0xEE5 drops, +0xE9C records the frame ([0x01447A2C]). When it changes, every open screen gets vtable slot 24 (+0x60), which for the HUD is FUN_008C54E0. About twenty HUD functions test +0xEE5.
    2. The player has the feature unlocked. FUN_008C64D0 (from slot 24, from OnOpen, and from the FeatureUnlockData UI message handler FUN_008C6620) shows the hotbar (hud+0x2CA) only if +0xEE5 is set, the frame is past +0xE9C + 1, and IsFeatureUnlocked(Hotbar). The hotkeys strip (hud+0x1BD) needs Hotkeys. OnOpen (FUN_008C4CB0) also caches ChargeBar (+0x2C5) and HealthBar (+0x2C6).

    IsFeatureUnlocked(enum) (FUN_00C42220, ECX = the local player's feature component, hud+0x308) hashes the enum name with the 32-bit UI hash (FUN_006E4CA0) and binary-searches the component's set at +0x24 (FUN_00C42320 -> FUN_00C2BFF0). The server must send the CRCs sorted ascending. An unsorted set looks complete in memory, but most lookups miss, so the HUD stayed hidden (fixed 2026-10-12).

    The component (vtable 0x01331D74) also keeps the previously announced set at +0x40. Slot 8 (FUN_00B29730) posts one FeatureUnlockData {feature enum, 1} per newly added feature (FUN_00B297B0, only while the owning entity is local: entity+0x81 & 8). It skips the very first set it sees. The UI message bus is synchronous (FUN_00C1F930 calls each subscriber in turn; subscribe with FUN_00C1F8D0).

    HUD animation names are 32-bit UI hashes. Reversed so far: Initialise, Hide, HealthBarFlash, HitFadeOut, ShowPowerMarker, HidePowerMarker, string_cursorMode, showString_cursorMode, hideString_cursorMode.

    Feature enum (client order)

    The name table is at 0x01405278 (FUN_00BE86D0). This index is what IsFeatureUnlocked takes. The server sends names, so the order only matters when reading the client.

    RPC reference

    #0 RPCQuitGame both handled

    Ordinal0 (0x0)
    Client senderRPC_QuitGame_Send 0x00717B40
    Client receive stubFUN_007fbcf0 0x007FBCF0
    Emulator handlerQuitGame.Handle
    Emulator classQuitGame Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/QuitGame.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 7× (C2S, S2C)

    Empty payload.

    RPCQuitGame, ordinal 0, no payload, both directions.

    Server to client. Reader: FUN_007FBCF0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0, ...). Verified empty: the stub reads nothing and dispatches straight to the handler at 0x007D26E0, which only sets ClientNet +0xE8 = 1. The client-net tick FUN_007D0430 then (once +0xEC >= +0xF0) sets DAT_01440E98[0x70] = 1, the "Level unload" flag the gameplay tick FUN_00964010 polls, and bumps the "ShuttingDown" stat. The world unloads and the client exits.

    Client to server: sent by the pause menu's Quit button after its confirm dialog (ui_pause_menu_v3 FUN_008FAD80, button hash "Quit" -> confirm callback FUN_008FB010 -> send FUN_00717B40). The client does nothing else by itself; it waits for the echo. Without it the button did nothing visible (2026-10-12: three QuitGame in the engine log, nothing until Alt+F4).

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.QuitGame);

    return bitStream;
}
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    connection.SaveState();

    connection.Send(new QuitGame());

    Console.WriteLine("[QuitGame] Client asked to quit - state saved, QuitGame echoed back.");

    return true;
}

    #1 RPCSentErrorToClient S2C implemented

    Ordinal1 (0x1)
    Client receive stubFUN_007fdb00 0x007FDB00
    Emulator classSentErrorToClient Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SentErrorToClient.cs

    Ordinal 1, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FDB00 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 1, ...).

    ErrorCode    32 bits
Detail       32 bits
Title        string
Message      string

    The two numbers are full-width reads through the FUN_006FB780 path, so they go out big-endian. The two strings use FUN_007EA530, the same reader as every other string in the protocol, so WriteString handles them.

    The stub sent the id and nothing else, so the client read four fields' worth of stale buffer whenever this was used. Field names are by role - the logger's name pointers do not survive decompilation in this stub - but the count, order and widths come straight from the reader.

    FieldTypeNotes
    ErrorCodeuint
    Detailuint
    Titlestring?
    Messagestring?
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SentErrorToClient);

    WriteUInt32(bitStream, ErrorCode);
    WriteUInt32(bitStream, Detail);

    bitStream.WriteString(Title);
    bitStream.WriteString(Message);

    return bitStream;
}

    #2 RPCShutdownMessage S2C implemented

    Ordinal2 (0x2)
    Client receive stubFUN_00800360 0x00800360
    Emulator classShutdownMessage Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ShutdownMessage.cs

    Ordinal 2, server to client. Reader: FUN_00800360 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 2, ...).

    Reason    32 bits
Seconds   32 bits

    Both are read at full width through the FUN_006FB780 path, so both are byte-swapped on the way in and have to go out big-endian - same rule as MapSpec.Seed and every other full-width field in this project. The stub used to send the id alone, which left the client reading two words of whatever followed in the buffer.

    The handler takes the two values in this order; what they mean is not recorded anywhere we can read, so the names are by role. A countdown message with a reason code and a delay is the obvious shape, but do not treat the names as established.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Reasonuint
    Secondsuint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.ShutdownMessage);

    WriteUInt32(bitStream, Reason);
    WriteUInt32(bitStream, Seconds);

    return bitStream;
}

    #3 RPCClientConnected C2S handled

    Ordinal3 (0x3)
    Client senderFUN_00946d60 0x00946D60
    Emulator handlerClientConnected.Handle
    Emulator classClientConnected Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ClientConnected.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 5× (C2S)

    FieldJSON type
    characterUUIDstring
    clientTestAppTypeint
    clientVersionNumberstring
    connectInvisiblybool
    instanceIPAddressstring
    Example payload
    {
  "clientVersionNumber": "20335948d8ece3bebf9e33bd936bef90",
  "connectInvisibly": true,
  "clientTestAppType": 0,
  "instanceIPAddress": "127.0.0.1",
  "characterUUID": ""
}
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var sessionTicket = bitStream.ReadString();

    var unknownFieldBuffer = new byte[2];
    bitStream.ReadBits(unknownFieldBuffer, 9, alignBitsToRight: true);

    var gameHost = bitStream.ReadString();

    var trailingBitBuffer = new byte[1];
    bitStream.ReadBits(trailingBitBuffer, 1, alignBitsToRight: true);

    Console.WriteLine($"[ClientConnected] sessionTicket={sessionTicket} "
        + $"unknownField9bits=0x{unknownFieldBuffer[0]:X2}{unknownFieldBuffer[1]:X2} "
        + $"gameHost={gameHost} trailingBit=0x{trailingBitBuffer[0]:X2}");

    // Debug-attach stall: was hardcoded on for every connection (blocks real play testing).
    // Flip to true only when you actually need the x32dbg-attach window.
    // Not `const` on purpose - a const bool makes the compiler treat this whole `if` as
    // unreachable at compile time and emit CS0162 on the block's contents.
    bool attachDebuggerBeforeHandshake = false;
    if (attachDebuggerBeforeHandshake)
    {
        const int debugAttachDelaySeconds = 5;
        Console.WriteLine($"[ClientConnected] Sleeping {debugAttachDelaySeconds}s "
            + "before sending ServerInfo/MapDefinition - attach x32dbg now.");
        Thread.Sleep(debugAttachDelaySeconds * 1000);
        Console.WriteLine("[ClientConnected] Delay done, continuing.");
    }

    // Util.CharacterUuid() hands back a string from the web service, which is not guaranteed
    // to parse. A null ServerOwnerGUID is a legal value on the wire (presence bit 0), so fall
    // back to that rather than inventing a Guid the web service does not know about.
    Guid? serverOwnerGuid = Guid.TryParse(Util.CharacterUuid(), out var parsedOwnerGuid)
        ? parsedOwnerGuid
        : null;

    var serverInfo = new ServerInfo
    {
        ServerOwnerGuid = serverOwnerGuid,
        WorldUuid = WorldUuid,
        ServerUuid = ServerUuid,

        ServerOwnerName = Util.CharacterName(),

        ChatHost = "127.0.0.1",
        ChatPort = 444,

        // Client clamps both to 32.
        MaxUsers = 32,
        MinUsersRequiredToPlay = 1,

        GameModeEntityId = 0,

        IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayers = false
    };

    var serverInfoStream = serverInfo.Serialize();

    Console.WriteLine($"[ClientConnected] Sending ServerInfo: "
        + $"ServerOwnerGUID={serverInfo.ServerOwnerGuid?.ToString() ?? "<none>"} "
        + $"WorldUUID={serverInfo.WorldUuid} ServerUUID={serverInfo.ServerUuid} "
        + $"ServerOwnerName='{serverInfo.ServerOwnerName}' "
        + $"ChatHost='{serverInfo.ChatHost}' ChatPort={serverInfo.ChatPort} "
        + $"MaxUsers={serverInfo.MaxUsers} MinUsers={serverInfo.MinUsersRequiredToPlay} "
        + $"GameModeEntityID={serverInfo.GameModeEntityId} "
        + $"({serverInfoStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed()} bytes, "
        + $"{serverInfoStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed()} bits used)");

    connection.Send(serverInfoStream);

    var definition = connection.WorldManager.Definition;

    Console.WriteLine($"[ClientConnected] Sending MapDefinition: "
        + $"Searchable=[{string.Join(",", definition.Spec.Searchable)}] "
        + $"AdventureType='{definition.AdventureType}' MapFileName='{definition.MapFileName}' "
        + $"FeatureName='{definition.Spec.FeatureName}' Seed={definition.Spec.Seed} "
        + $"GameMode={definition.GameMode}");

    var mapDefinitionStream = definition.Serialize();
    mapDefinitionStream.PrintHex(out var mapDefinitionHex);
    Console.WriteLine($"[ClientConnected] MapDefinition raw bytes "
        + $"({mapDefinitionStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed()} bytes, "
        + $"{mapDefinitionStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed()} bits used): {mapDefinitionHex}");

    connection.Send(mapDefinitionStream);

    // ResourceCheck (ordinal 10) used to go out here. It does not belong on this side of the
    // wire: the client has no receive stub for ordinal 10 at all, only a sender
    // (FUN_008260D0, which opens an outgoing RPC via FUN_00BC56B0(10, ...) and writes a
    // length plus a byte array). Anything the server sent under that ordinal was dropped by
    // the dispatcher without a word. If ResourceCheck is needed, it is the client that asks
    // and the server that answers - it belongs in PlayerConnection.ProcessPacket.

    var setConnectionTimeout = new SetConnectionTimeout { Millis = 30000 };

    connection.Send(setConnectionTimeout);

    Console.WriteLine("[ClientConnected] Sent SetConnectionTimeout: Millis=30000");

    return true;
}

    #5 RPCClientReadyToPlay C2S handled

    Ordinal5 (0x5)
    Client senderFUN_0080ff20 0x0080FF20
    Emulator handlerClientReadyToPlay.Handle
    Emulator classClientReadyToPlay Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ClientReadyToPlay.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 5× (C2S)

    Empty payload.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    Debug.WriteLine("", nameof(ClientReadyToPlay));

    // UnlockPosition (ordinal 157) used to be sent here, between SetClientEntity and
    // PlayerSpawned. It is a client-to-server packet: the client has no receive stub for 157,
    // only a sender (FUN_007E5770, which opens an outgoing RPC via FUN_00BC56B0(0x9D, ...)
    // with an empty body). Every copy the server sent was dropped by the dispatcher.
    connection.Send(new SetClientEntity { EntityId = connection.PlayerEntity.Id });

    // PlayerSpawned carries the spawn transform. It used to go out empty, which left the
    // client on its own primed defaults - position (0, -0x3FC0, 0) and both angles at
    // -0x3200 - no matter where the server had actually placed the player.
    var playerSpawned = new PlayerSpawned
    {
        EntityId = connection.PlayerEntity.Id
    };

    if (connection.PlayerEntity.TryGetComponent<SmoothedTransformComponent>(out var transform))
    {
        playerSpawned.Position = transform.Position;
        playerSpawned.YawDegrees = transform.YawDegrees;
    }

    connection.Send(playerSpawned);

    Console.WriteLine($"[ClientReadyToPlay] Sent SetClientLocalPlayerEntity(EntityId={connection.PlayerEntity.Id}), "
        + $"PlayerSpawned(Position={playerSpawned.Position}, Yaw={playerSpawned.YawDegrees}, "
        + $"Pitch={playerSpawned.PitchDegrees}).");

    // TeleportBegins here instead of at EnteredGameplay.
    //
    // Sending it from EnteredGameplay is proven to work: ModeGlobal+0xFC4 went from
    // 0x0/format=3 to "2x79 format=0 VALID", ui_loading_screen's +0x194 gate cleared, and the
    // game-state flags reached 0x17 for the first time. But it lands after the client is
    // already in gameplay, so the client teleports properly - disconnects, reconnects - and we
    // pay an extra round trip for a world it was already in.
    //
    // ClientReadyToPlay is ~10 s earlier and before gameplay entry, so if the ordinal-35
    // handler already exists at this point the initial load becomes the teleport and there is
    // no second connection. If it does not exist yet the packet is simply dropped, exactly as
    // it was when we tried ClientConnected - harmless, and the image staying INVALID in the
    // tool will say so.
    if (SendTeleportHere && !_teleportSent)
    {
        _teleportSent = true;

        var definition = connection.WorldManager.Definition;

        // One field. FUN_00964EE0 reads a MapSpec via FUN_007E9810 and stops - the six extra
        // fields this used to carry were never read, and neither was the trailing float.
        //
        // That float was not arbitrary: it stopped a real STATUS_FLOAT_MULTIPLE_TRAPS at
        // 0x00D45EDC. But the cause was MapSpec.Encode being short of nonSearchable,
        // masteryLevels and version at the time, so the client overran the end of the spec and
        // a zero reached a divisor in FUN_00D45E60. The spec is complete now; there is nothing
        // left to overrun, and the workaround goes with the bug.
        var teleportBegins = new TeleportBegins
        {
            Spec = definition.Spec
        };

        var teleportStream = teleportBegins.Serialize();

        Console.WriteLine($"[ClientReadyToPlay] Sending TeleportBegins (ordinal 35), "
            + $"{teleportStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed()} bytes.");

        connection.Send(teleportStream);
    }

    var entityAdd = new EntityAdd
    {
        Id = connection.PlayerEntity.Id,
        NameHash = Util.ComputeCrc32(connection.PlayerEntity.Name),
        SyncData = connection.PlayerEntity.GetSyncData(newEntity: true)
    };

    connection.PlayerConnectionManager.BroadcastToAllExcept(connection.Guid.g, entityAdd);

    return true;
}

        "region": "",
        "adventure": "Home_Island_Adventure",
        "difficulty": "Easy",

    "GameMode": 1,
    "Seed": 0
  }
}

    Field order and widths match the client (reader FUN_007E9E80, names from FUN_00805F80): MapSpec, AdventureType, MapFileName, GroupLock, GameQueueCRC, GameMode, and one unnamed 32-bit field.

    The only thing that was wrong here was byte order. GameQueueCRC and Unnamed are full-width 32-bit fields, and the client byte-swaps those (FUN_006FB780 path), so they have to go out big-endian - the same way ChunkSync already writes its data lengths. Ranged fields such as GameMode are read through the opposite predicate and stay little-endian.

    FieldTypeNotes
    MapSizeChunksVector3Int
    SpecMapSpec
    AdventureTypestring
    MapFileNamestring
    GroupLockGuid?
    GameQueueCRCuint?
    GameModeuint
    Unnameduint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.MapDefinition);

    Spec.Encode(bitStream);

    bitStream.WriteString(AdventureType);
    bitStream.WriteString(MapFileName);

    bitStream.WriteGuid(GroupLock);

    bitStream.WriteOptional(GameQueueCRC, (value) =>
    {
        WriteUInt32(bitStream, value);
    });

    bitStream.WriteRanged(GameMode, GameModeMax);

    WriteUInt32(bitStream, Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    #9 RPCFrameTimeSyncCheck S2C implemented

    Ordinal9 (0x9)
    Emulator classFrameTimeSyncCheck Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/FrameTimeSyncCheck.cs

    Ordinal 9. DEAD IN THIS BUILD - do not send it and do not expect to receive it.

    Every RPC the client knows has a stub ending in FUN_00BB3E50(direction, ordinal, ...). Walking all 263 call sites, ordinal 9 appears in neither direction: the client can neither send nor receive it. The name survives in the ordinal table at 0x01409528 (entry 9 of 341), so the RPC was declared and then compiled out of this build.

    The class is kept so the enum entry has a home and so the next person does not spend an evening implementing it. Anything sent under this id is dropped by the dispatcher without a word, exactly like ResourceCheck and UnlockPosition were.

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.FrameTimeSyncCheck);

    return bitStream;
}

    #10 RPCResourceCheck C2S handled

    Ordinal10 (0xA)
    Client senderFUN_008260d0 0x008260D0
    Emulator handlerResourceCheckReceived.Handle
    Emulator classResourceCheck Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ResourceCheck.cs
    Emulator classResourceCheckReceived Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ResourceCheckReceived.cs
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.ResourceCheck);

    return bitStream;
}

    Handles ordinal 10 (RPCResourceCheck) arriving FROM the client.

    This used to be sent by the server, which was backwards. The client has no receive stub for ordinal 10 - only a sender, FUN_008260D0, which opens an outgoing RPC via FUN_00BC56B0(10, ...) and then writes a 32-bit length followed by that many bytes. Everything the server sent under this id was dropped by the dispatcher without a word.

    So the direction is client to server, and this is the missing half. It logs the payload and nothing more: the length prefix is readable, but what the bytes are - a manifest, a set of hashes, a file list - is not established, and inventing a reply for a request we cannot read is how the 271 crash happened.

    If this never fires, that is also worth knowing. Across every session logged so far the client has only ever sent ordinals 3, 4, 5, 6, 29 and 30, so ResourceCheck may simply not be used in this flow.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    // Ordinal 10 is below 121, so the id took a single byte on the wire.
    var remainingBits = (uint)Math.Max(0, totalBits - 8);
    var payload = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(payload, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);

    var length = payload.Length >= 4
        ? (payload[0] << 24) | (payload[1] << 16) | (payload[2] << 8) | payload[3]
        : -1;

    Console.WriteLine($"[ResourceCheck] Received (ordinal 10) from guid={connection.Guid.g:X}. "
        + $"totalBits={totalBits} payloadBits={remainingBits} readOk={readOk} "
        + $"lengthPrefix={length} raw: {string.Join(" ", payload.Take(64).Select(b => b.ToString("x2")))}");

    return true;
}

    #12 RPCBeginSync S2C implemented

    Ordinal12 (0xC)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ec550 0x007EC550
    Emulator classBeginSync Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/BeginSync.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 5× (S2C)

    FieldJSON type
    numChunksToSyncint
    Example payload
    {
  "numChunksToSync": 144
}

    Reader: FUN_007EC550 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xC, ...), field read by FUN_007D72F0.

    One ranged field, maximum 0x1C00, so 13 bits, clamped to 0x1C00 on read. The server was writing it with a maximum of 0x8000 - 16 bits.

    This has been latent rather than fatal. With 16 chunks the whole value sits in the first byte, and because the reader right-aligns only the trailing partial byte, the client still came out with 16. It breaks above 255: 300 chunks would arrive as 44, because the second byte of the little-endian value falls outside the 13 bits the client takes. Nothing rides on the three extra bits themselves - BeginSync carries no other field - so only the value was ever at risk.

    FieldTypeNotes
    NumChunksToSyncint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.BeginSync);

    var clamped = Math.Min((uint)NumChunksToSync, NumChunksMax);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(clamped, NumChunksMax);

    return bitStream;
}

    #13 RPCChunkSync S2C implemented

    Ordinal13 (0xD)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ee220 0x007EE220
    Emulator classChunkSync Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ChunkSync.cs

    Reader: FUN_007EE220 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xD, ...) coords FUN_007D6A30 X ranged max 32 (6 bits), Y ranged max 7 (3 bits), Z max 32 Data1 FUN_007E7DB0 -> FUN_007D6E60 presence bit, 32-bit length, then length bytes Data2 same Adjacent FUN_007D6DF0 presence bit, then 7 bits ranged max 64, clamped

    Coords and both arrays already matched. The one thing that did not: AdjacentChunks was going out as a raw 8-bit write, and the client reads 7 bits (8 - NumBitsRequiredByte(0x40)) and clamps the result to 64. It never bit us because the field has always been null, so only the absent-bit went out - but the moment it carries a value the stream would slip by one bit and every chunk after it would be garbage.

    The 32-bit lengths stay big-endian. That is not a workaround: full-width fields are read through FUN_006FB780, which byte-swaps while DAT_01449B5E is zero, and it is zero in this build. These lengths are the evidence for that - the client accepts all 16 chunks - which is what pinned the byte order for MapSpec.Seed and the rest.

    FieldTypeNotes
    CoordsVector3Int
    Data1byte[]?
    Data2byte[]?
    AdjacentChunksbyte?
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.ChunkSync);

    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)Coords.X, MaxAxisXZ);
    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)Coords.Y, MaxAxisY);
    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)Coords.Z, MaxAxisXZ);

    bitStream.WriteOptional(Data1, (value) =>
    {
        WriteLength(bitStream, value.Length);
        bitStream.WriteBits(value, (uint)value.Length * 8, true);
    });

    bitStream.WriteOptional(Data2, (value) =>
    {
        WriteLength(bitStream, value.Length);
        bitStream.WriteBits(value, (uint)value.Length * 8, true);
    });

    bitStream.WriteOptional(AdjacentChunks, (value) =>
    {
        bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(value, AdjacentChunksMax), AdjacentChunksMax);
    });

    return bitStream;
}

    #14 RPCPartialChunkEditsSync S2C implemented

    Ordinal14 (0xE)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f8a60 0x007F8A60
    Emulator classPartialChunkEditsSync Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PartialChunkEditsSync.cs

    Ordinal 14, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F8A60 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xE, ...).

    ChunkCoord     FUN_007D6A30   X 6 bits (max 32), Y 3 bits (max 7), Z 6 bits (max 32)
ChunkEditList  FUN_007E79B0   count, then that many entries via FUN_007EA150
Unnamed bit    FUN_00EA6FA0   1 bit

    Four things were wrong.

    1. Y was written 6 bits wide. The coordinate reader here is FUN_007D6A30 - the very same one ChunkSync uses - and it reads Y as a ranged value with maximum 7, so 3 bits. Every packet was three bits too long from the first field onwards.

    2. The list count was written with maximum 7. FUN_007D7140 reads it with maximum 8 and takes the escape branch when the value comes back as exactly 8, so the field is 4 bits.

    3. The count was written as Count - 1. Nothing on the reading side adds it back, so every list arrived one entry short.

    4. The trailing bit was not written at all.

    NOT VERIFIED: the layout of an individual edit. The client reads them with FUN_007EA150 into 0x14-byte slots, which is a useful cross-check if ChunkEdit.Serialize ever gets revisited, but that class is untouched here and its field layout is still a guess.

    FieldTypeNotes
    ChunkCoordVector3Int
    ChunkEditListList<ChunkEdit>
    Unnamedbool

    Trailing flag. Purpose unknown - the reader takes one bit and names nothing.

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.PartialChunkEditsSync);

    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)ChunkCoord.X, MaxAxisXZ);
    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)ChunkCoord.Y, MaxAxisY);
    bitStream.WriteRanged((uint)ChunkCoord.Z, MaxAxisXZ);

    var count = (uint)ChunkEditList.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, ChunkEditCountMax), ChunkEditCountMax);

    if (count >= ChunkEditCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();

        var countBytes = BitConverter.GetBytes(count);
        Array.Reverse(countBytes);
        bitStream.WriteBits(countBytes, 32, true);
    }

    foreach (var chunkEdit in ChunkEditList)
        chunkEdit.Serialize(bitStream);

    bitStream.Write(Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    #15 RPCRequestEquipInventoryItem C2S handled

    Ordinal15 (0xF)
    Client senderFUN_00956a20 0x00956A20
    Emulator handlerRequestEquipInventoryItem.Handle
    Emulator classRequestEquipInventoryItem Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/RequestEquipInventoryItem.cs
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!ReadLocation(bitStream, out var location)) return false;

    if (!bitStream.Read(out int inventoryOwner)) return false;

    if(!bitStream.ReadInt32(45, out var slotId)) return false;

    var fromUserCommand = bitStream.ReadBit();
    var findSuitableSlot = bitStream.ReadBit();

    Debug.WriteLine($"location {location}, inventoryOwner {inventoryOwner}, slotId {slotId}, fromUserCommand {fromUserCommand}, findSuitableSlot {findSuitableSlot}", nameof(RequestEquipInventoryItem));

    if (connection.PlayerEntity.TryGetComponent<ClientInventoryComponent>(out var clientInventoryComponent))
    {
        var itemId = clientInventoryComponent.InventoryEntityList[slotId];
        connection.WorldManager.EntityManager.TryGetEntity(itemId, out var itemEntity);

        if (itemEntity!.TryGetComponent<InventoryItemComponent>(out var inventoryItemComponent))
        {
            var blockID = BlockCrcMapping.GetBlockId(inventoryItemComponent.InventorySlotData.Name ?? 0);
            if (location == ActionLocation.LeftHand)
            {
                connection.LeftHandBlockId = blockID;
            }
            else if (location == ActionLocation.RightHand)
            {
                connection.RightHandBlockId = blockID;
            }
        }
    }

    return true;
}

    return true;

        + $"timeMode={timeMode} questSort={questSort}");

    Client receive stubFUN_007fe9f0 0x007FE9F0
    Emulator classSetEndGameList Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetEndGameList.cs

    Ordinal 25, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FE9F0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x19, ...).

    Entries   FUN_007E7220   count via FUN_007D7020: ranged max 4 -> 3 bits, escape at 4
                         (1 bit + 32 bits); then count x FUN_007E9190
Unnamed   32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    Each entry, from FUN_007E9190:

    Name      string
ValueA    1 presence bit + 32 bits
ValueB    1 presence bit + 32 bits
Rank      ranged, max 3 -> 2 bits
ScoreA    32 bits, full width -> big-endian
ScoreB    32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    Names by role - a name, two optional numbers, a small rank and two scores is what an end-of-game scoreboard row looks like, but the loggers give up nothing here.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntriesList<Entry>
    Unnameduint
    Entryclass
    Namestring?
    ValueAuint?
    ValueBuint?
    Rankuint
    ScoreAint
    ScoreBint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SetEndGameList);

    var count = (uint)Entries.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, EntryCountMax), EntryCountMax);

    if (count >= EntryCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var entry in Entries)
    {
        bitStream.WriteString(entry.Name);

        InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, entry.ValueA);
        InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, entry.ValueB);

        bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(entry.Rank, RankMax), RankMax);

        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)entry.ScoreA);
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)entry.ScoreB);
    }

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    #26 RPCSetMapRotationList S2C implemented

    Ordinal26 (0x1A)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ff210 0x007FF210
    Emulator classSetMapRotationList Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetMapRotationList.cs

    Ordinal 26, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FF210 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x1A, ...).

    Specs     FUN_007E7480   count via FUN_007D7380: ranged max 0x20 -> 6 bits, escape at 32
                         (1 bit + 32 bits); then count x FUN_007E9810
Unnamed   32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    FUN_007E9810 is the MapSpec reader - the same one MapDefinition and TeleportBegins go through. So this is literally a list of map specs, which is what a rotation is.

    An empty list is legal and costs 6 bits.

    FieldTypeNotes
    SpecsList<MapSpec>
    Unnameduint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SetMapRotationList);

    var count = (uint)Specs.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, SpecCountMax), SpecCountMax);

    if (count >= SpecCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var spec in Specs)
        spec.Encode(bitStream);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    #27 RPCSetUGCIslandListForFTUE S2C implemented

    Ordinal27 (0x1B)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ffc80 0x007FFC80
    Emulator classSetUGCIslandListForFTUE Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetUGCIslandListForFTUE.cs

    Ordinal 27, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FFC80 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x1B, ...).

    A single list, read by FUN_007E5E40:

    count      FUN_007D7510   ranged, max 0x400 -> 11 bits, escape when it reads exactly 1024
escape     1 bit, then a full 32-bit count
entries    count x FUN_007EA3B0

    FUN_007EA3B0 is the same reader ServerInfo uses for ServerOwnerGUID and the rest: one presence bit, then - only if set - sixteen bytes as four 32-bit words. So this is a list of UUIDs, which fits the name: the islands offered during the FTUE.

    The elements go out through the same helper shape as ServerInfo's UUIDs. Byte order inside a UUID only affects how it prints; what matters for the stream is the width, 129 bits present or 1 bit absent.

    An empty list is a complete, legal message and costs 11 bits.

    FieldTypeNotes
    IslandUuidsList<Guid?>
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SetUGCIslandListForFTUE);

    var count = (uint)IslandUuids.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, IslandCountMax), IslandCountMax);

    if (count >= IslandCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();

        var countBytes = BitConverter.GetBytes(count);
        Array.Reverse(countBytes);
        bitStream.WriteBits(countBytes, 32, true);
    }

    foreach (var uuid in IslandUuids)
        WriteUuid(bitStream, uuid);

    return bitStream;
}

    #28 RPCSetSystemCharacterMap S2C implemented

    Ordinal28 (0x1C)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ff910 0x007FF910
    Emulator classSetSystemCharacterMap Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetSystemCharacterMap.cs

    Ordinal 28, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FF910 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x1C, ...), body read by FUN_007D8390.

    count    FUN_007D7140: ranged max 8 -> 4 bits, escape at 8 (1 bit + 32 bits)
keys     count x string        (read by FUN_007E7680)
values   count x (string, string)

    Three strings per entry, and the grouping is not interleaved: every key is read first, then the pairs. That ordering was the open question when this packet was first deferred, and it is settled by FUN_0080BFE0 - it pre-sizes the second container to the key count before the loop, so FUN_0080C280 returns a constant and the loop runs exactly count times rather than chasing a container that grows underneath it.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntriesList<Entry>
    Entryclass
    Keystring?
    ValueAstring?
    ValueBstring?
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SetSystemCharacterMap);

    var count = (uint)Entries.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, EntryCountMax), EntryCountMax);

    if (count >= EntryCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var entry in Entries)
        bitStream.WriteString(entry.Key);

    foreach (var entry in Entries)
    {
        bitStream.WriteString(entry.ValueA);
        bitStream.WriteString(entry.ValueB);
    }

    return bitStream;

    #29 RPCRunActionList C2S handled

    Ordinal29 (0x1D)

    Handles ordinal 29 (RPCRunActionList) from the client.

    The old version read the payload, printed it, and returned. That is the whole bug this file is about: the client asks the server to run an action list, the server answers with nothing, and the action list never runs anywhere.

    Why that matters for the stuck FTUE, from GeoData.json. The only adventure in the game with IsFTUE:true is FTUE_The_Old_City (Index 78), and its cutscenes carry their consequences in OnCompleteActions.AlwaysActions. Eva_Leads_Player (AnimatedSequences Index 7) ends with:

    OldCity_Gladiator_1_HideNode
OldCity_GladiatorSpawner_1_Despawn
OldCity_GladiatorSpawner_2_Spawn
Trigger_Eva_Leads_Player_Part2_OnEntities
Unlock_OldCity_Tutorial_Move          <-- movement input

    and Rescue_Dilemma_Choice_Explorer (Index 9) ends with Unlock_OldCity_Tutorial_Attack, Unlock_OldCity_Reach_ExplorerLeader and SetCheckpoint_OldCity_LeftWing. Nothing else in the data unlocks input. If the action lists never run, movement and attack stay locked and only ESC responds - which is exactly the reported symptom.

    Sequence 7 also declares FadeSettings { InitialState: "Black", FadeAtEnd: true } with UseCutsceneUI and ShowBars, so the fullscreen veil belongs to the cutscene, not to the loading screen. That is consistent with the census finding of fade widgets sitting Inactive with no anigraph: put up, never animated out.

    WHY ECHOING IS SAFE HERE, WHEN IT CRASHED FOR 271

    Echoing 268's payload into ordinal 271 produced STATUS_FLOAT_MULTIPLE_TRAPS, because those are two different packet shapes - 271's handler reads a float out of bits that were written by 268's writer. That failure does not apply here. These bits were produced by the client's own ordinal-29 writer, so the client's own ordinal-29 reader is symmetric with them by construction. Same ordinal, same layout.

    OBSERVED LAYOUT (four captures, one session)

    00 00 04 3c | 00 00 00 04 | 94 a4 2f 7e 7c 67 88 b7 | 28 more bytes
00 00 04 b2 | 00 00 00 01 | 94 a4 2f 7e 7c 67 88 b7 | ...
00 00 04 bc | 00 00 00 04 | 94 a4 2f 7e 7c 67 88 b7 | ...
00 00 04 c8 | 00 00 00 04 | 94 a4 2f 7e 7c 67 88 b7 | ...

    352 bits after the one-byte id. First u32 big-endian varies per call (1084, 1202, 1212, 1224) and is presumably the action-list id. Second u32 is an entity id: 4 is the player, 1 is HUB_NPC_Gladiator_OldCity - which is the entity Eva_Leads_Player names in its AdditionalPropData, so the pairing is consistent. The eight bytes after it were identical in all four captures. The remaining 28 bytes are not decoded and are not touched here.

    The fields above are only logged. Nothing is parsed out to build the reply, so a wrong guess about their meaning cannot corrupt what goes on the wire.

    NOT VERIFIED: that ordinal 29 is handled in the server-to-client direction at all. It has only ever been observed client-to-server. This is the experiment. Change one flag at a time, and Packets/RunActionList.cs stays unused on purpose - it serialises a string, which does not match anything above, and would be unparseable if it were ever sent.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    // Ordinal 29 is below 121, so the id occupied a single byte on the wire - unlike the
    // 268 family, which needs 16 bits for the 0xFF escape plus one byte.
    var remainingBits = (uint)Math.Max(0, totalBits - 8);
    var payload = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(payload, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);

    var hex = string.Join(" ", payload.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    var actionListId = payload.Length >= 4
        ? (payload[0] << 24) | (payload[1] << 16) | (payload[2] << 8) | payload[3]
        : -1;

    var entityId = payload.Length >= 8
        ? (payload[4] << 24) | (payload[5] << 16) | (payload[6] << 8) | payload[7]
        : -1;

    var expectedId = connection.PlayerEntity.Id;
    var idNote = entityId == expectedId
        ? "the player entity"
        : $"NOT the player entity ({expectedId})";

    Console.WriteLine($"[RunActionList] Received (ordinal 29). totalBits={totalBits} "
        + $"payloadBits={remainingBits} readOk={readOk} raw: {hex}");
    Console.WriteLine($"[RunActionList] ActionListId={actionListId} EntityId={entityId} ({idNote}).");

    if (!readOk)
    {
        // Echoing bits we failed to read would put garbage on the wire. Drop instead, and
        // say so, rather than sending something the client has to survive.
        Console.WriteLine("[RunActionList] Payload read FAILED - not echoing.");

        return true;
    }

    if (EchoToRequester)
    {
        connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.RunActionList,
            Payload = payload,
            PayloadBits = remainingBits
        });

        Console.WriteLine($"[RunActionList] Echoed ordinal 29 back to the requester, "
            + $"{remainingBits} payload bits.");
    }

    if (StartAnimationSequence && entityId > 0)
    {
        var sequencePayload = new byte[]
        {
            (byte)(entityId >> 24),
            (byte)(entityId >> 16),
            (byte)(entityId >> 8),
            (byte)entityId,
            SequenceIndexByte
        };

        connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.AnimationSequenceStarting,
            Payload = sequencePayload,
            PayloadBits = (uint)sequencePayload.Length * 8
        });

        Console.WriteLine($"[RunActionList] Sent AnimationSequenceStarting (ordinal 272) "
            + $"unprompted for EntityId={entityId}, sequence index {SequenceIndexByte >> 3}, "
            + $"payload {string.Join(" ", sequencePayload.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")))}.");
    }

    if (BroadcastToOthers)
    {
        connection.PlayerConnectionManager.BroadcastToAllExcept(connection.Guid.g, new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.RunActionList,
            Payload = payload,
            PayloadBits = remainingBits
        });

        Console.WriteLine("[RunActionList] Forwarded ordinal 29 to the other connections.");
    }

    return true;
}

    #30 RPCEnteredGameplay C2S handled

    Ordinal30 (0x1E)
    Client senderFUN_0080f1b0 0x0080F1B0
    Emulator handlerEnteredGameplay.Handle
    Emulator classEnteredGameplay Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EnteredGameplay.cs
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();
    var remainingBits = (uint)(totalBits - 8);
    var buffer = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];
    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(buffer, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);
    var hex = string.Join(" ", buffer.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    Console.WriteLine($"[EnteredGameplay] Received. readOk={readOk} raw: {hex}");

    const string videoName = "GCIntro_StoryTextVideo";

    // The client selects the video by its index in its own Videos list, not by name and not
    // by hash. This used to send a CRC32 of the name, of which the client read the top two
    // bits as the index - so which video started was down to two bits of a hash.
    //
    // GeoData's Videos list has three entries and indices are 1-based, so GCIntro_
    // StoryTextVideo is 3. It is the looping one (SkySaga_IntroStoryBackground_Loop with
    // INTRO_STORY_TEXT over it) and its on-complete transition points at CGIntro_Video1.
    var playVideo = new PlayFullScreenVideo
    {
        VideoIndex = PlayFullScreenVideo.GCIntroStoryTextVideo
    };

    // Only read back by FullScreenVideoCompleted's log line, so it stays accurate about which
    // video the client is acknowledging.
    connection.CurrentVideoFileName = videoName;

    // TeleportBegins IS a teleport, not just a picture hint - see the note on _teleportSent.
    var teleporting = SendTeleport && !_teleportSent;

    if (teleporting)
    {
        _teleportSent = true;

        SendTeleportBegins(connection);

        // No video on the connection that is about to teleport. EnteredGameplay arrives twice
        // - once before the teleport and once on the connection that comes back after it -
        // and sending the video both times overlapped two playbacks, which is what the doubled
        // audio was. The first one would be torn down by the teleport seconds later anyway,
        // so the connection that stays is the one that should get it.
        Console.WriteLine("[EnteredGameplay] Teleporting - holding PlayFullScreenVideo until "
            + "the client comes back.");

        return true;
    }

    SendIsLocal(connection);

    // Debug switch: SKYSAGA_SKIP_INTRO=1 goes straight to the world. The client reads Esc through
    // raw input, so a posted key can't skip the video when testing unattended. Nothing on the
    // server depends on FullScreenVideoCompleted.
    // The intro video belongs to the FTUE only.
    if (!connection.WorldManager.Definition.Spec.IsFtue)
    {
        Console.WriteLine("[EnteredGameplay] Not the FTUE - no intro video.");
        return true;
    }

    if (Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("SKYSAGA_SKIP_INTRO") == "1")
    {
        Console.WriteLine("[EnteredGameplay] SKYSAGA_SKIP_INTRO=1 - not sending PlayFullScreenVideo.");
        return true;
    }

    var playVideoStream = playVideo.Serialize();

    connection.Send(playVideoStream);

    Console.WriteLine($"[EnteredGameplay] Sent PlayFullScreenVideo('{videoName}', index="
        + $"{playVideo.VideoIndex}, {playVideoStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed()} bytes, "
        + $"{playVideoStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed()} bits used).");

    return true;
}

    #32 RPCUpdateVoxelDamage S2C implemented

    Ordinal32 (0x20)
    Client receive stubFUN_00801c20 0x00801C20
    Emulator classUpdateVoxelDamage Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/UpdateVoxelDamage.cs

    Ordinal 32, server to client. Reader: FUN_00801C20 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x20, ...).

    DamageId    32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Amount      FUN_007D76B0: ranged, max 0x7FFF -> 15 bits
EntityId    32 bits, full width -> big-endian
SourceId    32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Position    FUN_007D66A0: X 17 bits, Y 15 bits biased by -0x3FC0, Z 17 bits
Timestamp   FUN_00C715B0: 64 bits -> big-endian

    This one sat unfinished for a while on FUN_00C715B0, which turns out to be nothing exotic: 0x40 bits with an eight-byte swap, the 64-bit sibling of the 32-bit full-width reads. The rest of that function formats a local value and reads nothing more.

    Position is the same reader PlayerSpawned, EventEffect and AoETriggered use - 64 units per world unit, narrow biased Y. Do not substitute the entity-sync encoding.

    FieldTypeNotes
    DamageIdint
    Amountuint
    EntityIdint
    SourceIdint
    PositionVector3
    Timestampulong
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.UpdateVoxelDamage);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)DamageId);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Amount, AmountMax), AmountMax);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)SourceId);

    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.X);
    WriteVertical(bitStream, Position.Y);
    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.Z);

    PvPWaitingInfo.WriteUInt64(bitStream, Timestamp);

    return bitStream;

    #35 RPCTeleportBegins S2C implemented

    Ordinal35 (0x23)
    Client receive stubRPC_TeleportBegins_Receive 0x00964EE0
    Emulator classTeleportBegins Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/TeleportBegins.cs

    Ordinal 35, server to client. Reader: FUN_00964EE0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x23, ...).

    The whole body is ONE MapSpec:

    FUN_00C0CC30();                    // construct a MapSpec
FUN_007E9810(param_2, local_544);  // read it
// nothing else is read - what follows is only the logging block, and it prints the
// result through FUN_007BA0C0, the MapSpec logger

    No MapSizeChunks, no AdventureType, no MapFileName, no GroupLock, no GameQueueCRC, no GameMode, no trailing word. This packet is not a copy of MapDefinition's body; it just happens to start with the same field.

    The old version wrote six extra fields after the spec. Those were never read - a receiver simply stops consuming - so they cost bandwidth and nothing else. The part that did hurt was the manually appended trailing float in EnteredGameplay: back when it was added, MapSpec.Encode was short of nonSearchable, masteryLevels and version, so the client ran off the end of the spec and into whatever followed, and a zero landed in a duration that FUN_00D45E60 divides by - hence STATUS_FLOAT_MULTIPLE_TRAPS at 0x00D45EDC. The float papered over an under-run.

    MapSpec is complete now, so the under-run is gone and the float has to go with it. Leaving it on is harmless to the parse but keeps a workaround alive for a bug that no longer exists.

    FieldTypeNotes
    SpecMapSpec
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.TeleportBegins);

    Spec.Encode(bitStream);

    return bitStream;
}
