    #37 RPCHitOccurred S2C implemented

    Ordinal37 (0x25)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f6010 0x007F6010
    Emulator classHitOccurred Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/HitOccurred.cs

    Ordinal 37, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F6010 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x25, ...).

    TargetEntityId   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
SourceEntityId   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Amount           FUN_007D69B0: ranged, max 0x7FFF -> 15 bits
Kind             ranged, max 9 -> 4 bits
Unnamed          32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Result           FUN_007E7E70: 1 bit, and if set an 8-bit ranged value, max 0x98

    The first five fields are the same opening HealOccurred and KillOccurred use. The last one held this packet up for a while: FUN_007E7E70 looked like a nested structure, but it is a plain optional - one bit, then the same 8-bit code CombatResult carries, read by the same FUN_007E8BA0. The FUN_0077A510 call that sits between them reads nothing at all; neither it nor any of its five callees touches the bitstream.

    FieldTypeNotes
    TargetEntityIdint
    SourceEntityIdint
    Amountuint
    Kinduint
    Unnamedint
    Resultuint?

    Null writes a single clear bit.

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.HitOccurred);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)TargetEntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)SourceEntityId);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Amount, AmountMax), AmountMax);
    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Kind, KindMax), KindMax);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)Unnamed);

    if (Result is null)
    {
        bitStream.Write0();
    }
    else
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Result.Value, ResultMax), ResultMax);
    }

    return bitStream;
}

Kind             ranged, max 9 -> 4 bits
Unnamed          32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    Five fields, in that order. The widths come from the reader; the names are by role, since the logger's name pointers are lost to the packed thunks in this stub. Two entity ids followed by an amount is the obvious reading of a heal event, but treat the names as provisional and the layout as solid.

    FieldTypeNotes
    TargetEntityIdint
    SourceEntityIdint
    Amountuint
    Kinduint
    Unnamedint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.HealOccurred);

    WriteInt32(bitStream, TargetEntityId);
    WriteInt32(bitStream, SourceEntityId);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Amount, AmountMax), AmountMax);
    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Kind, KindMax), KindMax);

    WriteInt32(bitStream, Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    #44 RPCAlterActivePhaseByFactor S2C implemented

    Ordinal44 (0x2C)
    Client receive stubFUN_007eb3e0 0x007EB3E0
    Emulator classAlterActivePhaseByFactor Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/AlterActivePhaseByFactor.cs

    Ordinal 44, server to client. Reader: FUN_007EB3E0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x2C, ...).

    Factor    FUN_00CC1740   ranged, max 0x20 -> 6 bits, clamped to 0..32
EntityId  inline         32 bits, full width, so big-endian

    The client scales the first field by DAT_012C1214, which reads 0x3D000000 - exactly 0.03125, the same constant PlayerSpawned uses for its angles. So the wire value 0..32 becomes a factor of 0.0 to 1.0 in steps of a thirty-second, which is what the name says it is.

    Set Factor as the real number; the conversion and the clamp happen here.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Factorfloat

    0.0 to 1.0.

    EntityIdint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.AlterActivePhaseByFactor);

    var units = (uint)Math.Clamp((int)(Factor / FactorScale), 0, (int)FactorMax);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(units, FactorMax);

    var idBytes = BitConverter.GetBytes(EntityId);
    Array.Reverse(idBytes);
    bitStream.WriteBits(idBytes, 32, true);

    return bitStream;
}

    #50 RPCEventEffect S2C implemented

    Ordinal50 (0x32)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f3280 0x007F3280
    Emulator classEventEffect Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EventEffect.cs

    Ordinal 50, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F3280 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x32, ...).

    EffectIndex   ranged, max 0xD0 -> 8 bits
EntityId      32 bits, full width -> big-endian
SourceId      32 bits, full width -> big-endian
ParamA        FUN_007D8110   16 bits
ParamB        FUN_007D8110   16 bits
Position      FUN_007D66A0   X 17 bits, Y 15 bits biased by -0x3FC0, Z 17 bits
Direction     FUN_007D6590   three components, ranged max 0x80 -> 8 bits each
Magnitude     FUN_007D76B0   ranged, max 0x7FFF -> 15 bits

    FUN_007D66A0 is the same position reader PlayerSpawned uses, so the scale is 64 units per world unit and Y is the narrow, biased one. Do not copy the entity-sync encoding here.

    Between the position and the direction the stub calls FUN_0077A330, which reads nothing at all - it copies three ints and clamps each to +-0x40. That clamp is why Direction is treated as a biased value here: the wire carries 0..128 and the client works in -64..+64.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EffectIndexuint
    EntityIdint
    SourceIdint
    ParamAushort
    ParamBushort
    PositionVector3
    DirectionVector3Int

    Each component is clamped to -64..+64 by the client.

    Magnitudeuint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.EventEffect);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(EffectIndex, EffectIndexMax), EffectIndexMax);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)SourceId);

    bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(ParamA), 16, true);
    bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(ParamB), 16, true);

    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.X);
    WriteVertical(bitStream, Position.Y);
    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.Z);

    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.X);
    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Y);
    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Z);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Magnitude, MagnitudeMax), MagnitudeMax);

    return bitStream;
}

      "Mouth_Human_Male02_Cute"
    ],
    "Backstory": "Human_Arden_Skysailor"
  }
}

    RPCSetCharacterCustomisationData, ordinal 53, client to server only (send stub FUN_00B21A10, no receive stub). Sent by the character creator (script action faGame_SetCharacterCustomisationData) on every change, not only at the end.

    Layout from the send stub: the 32-bit entity id (byte-swapped like every full-width field), then the CustomisationData struct written by FUN_00B22A00, the mirror of the Player binding reader FUN_00BFA830. 263 bits in all (client RPC stats). The struct is kept as raw bits.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var entityIdBytes = new byte[4];
    var data = new byte[(ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent.CustomisationDataBits + 7) / 8];

    var unread = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed() - (int)bitStream.GetReadOffset();

    if (unread < 32 + ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent.CustomisationDataBits
        || !bitStream.ReadBits(entityIdBytes, 32, true)
        || !bitStream.ReadBits(data, ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent.CustomisationDataBits, true))
    {
        Console.WriteLine($"[CharacterCreation] SetCharacterCustomisationData: short payload ({unread} bits), ignored.");

        return true;
    }

    // Kept on the connection, not applied to the entity: only the one in force at
    // SaveCharacterName counts.
    connection.CustomisationPayload = data;

    Console.WriteLine("[CharacterCreation] SetCharacterCustomisationData: "
        + string.Join(" ", data.Select(b => b.ToString("x2"))));

    return true;
}

    #69 RPCInventoryItemSwap C2S handled

    Ordinal69 (0x45)
    Client senderFUN_00995c20 0x00995C20
    Emulator handlerInventoryItemSwap.Handle
    Emulator classInventoryItemSwap Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/InventoryItemSwap.cs
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!bitStream.Read(out int sourceEntityID))
        return false;

    var inOutByteArray = new byte[4];

    if (!bitStream.ReadBits(inOutByteArray, 32 - Util.NumBitsRequiredUInt32(45), true))
        return false;

    var sourceSlotID = BitConverter.ToInt32(inOutByteArray, 0);

    if (!bitStream.Read(out int targetEntityID))
        return false;

    if (!bitStream.ReadBits(inOutByteArray, 32 - Util.NumBitsRequiredUInt32(45), true))
        return false;

    var targetSlotID = BitConverter.ToInt32(inOutByteArray, 0);

    Debug.WriteLine($"sourceEntityID: {sourceEntityID}, sourceSlotID: {sourceSlotID}, targetEntityID: {targetEntityID}, targetSlotID: {targetSlotID}", nameof(InventoryItemSwap));

    return true;
}

    Emulator classTimeSync Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/TimeSync.cs

    Ordinal 72, server to client. Reader: FUN_008012D0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x48, ...).

    Timestamp   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Phase       FUN_007D7860: ranged, max 0xF -> 4 bits
Unnamed     32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    Three fields in that order. Names are by role - two full words around a small counter is what a clock message looks like, but nothing here names itself.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Timestampuint
    Phaseuint
    Unnameduint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Unnamed);

    #73 RPCServerInfo S2C implemented

    Ordinal73 (0x49)
    Client receive stubFUN_007fdfd0 0x007FDFD0
    Emulator classServerInfo Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ServerInfo.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 5× (S2C)

    FieldJSON type
    serverInfoobject
    serverInfo.ChatHoststring
    serverInfo.ChatPortint
    serverInfo.GameModeEntityIDint
    serverInfo.IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayersbool
    serverInfo.MaxUsersint
    serverInfo.MinUsersRequiredToPlayint
    serverInfo.ServerOwnerGUIDstring
    serverInfo.ServerOwnerNamestring
    serverInfo.ServerUUIDstring
    serverInfo.WorldUUIDstring
    Example payload
    {
  "serverInfo": {
    "ServerOwnerGUID": "a7cfad0a-9634-4154-8b33-95425743f4ae",
    "WorldUUID": "6f0d3a1e-5c47-4f92-9a6b-1d84c0e73b25",
    "ServerUUID": "b3e94c07-2a15-4d68-8f31-7c05a9be4610",
    "ServerOwnerName": "NeUbivashka",
    "ChatHost": "127.0.0.1",
    "MaxUsers": 32,
    "MinUsersRequiredToPlay": 1,
    "ChatPort": 444,
    "GameModeEntityID": 0,
    "IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayers": false
  }
}

    Alpha 10 layout, read straight out of the client.

    Reader: FUN_007E9C60 Log/names: FUN_00805DD0 (prints every field with its real name) Stub: FUN_007FDFD0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x49, ...)

    Wire order and widths, exactly as the client consumes them:

    ServerOwnerGUID                       1 bit present + 4 x 32 bits
WorldUUID                             1 bit present + 4 x 32 bits
ServerUUID                            1 bit present + 4 x 32 bits
ServerOwnerName                       string
ChatHost                              string
MaxUsers                              ranged, max 32  -> 6 bits
MinUsersRequiredToPlay                ranged, max 32  -> 6 bits
ChatPort                              16 bits
GameModeEntityID                      32 bits
IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayers    1 bit

    The old alpha 3 shape (ServerBiome, ServerAdventureCrc, MapHeaderSeed, IsHomeWorld, IsMyWorld, and the GUIDs written as strings) does not exist in this build. Since this is the first packet the client receives after connecting, every one of those fields shifted the whole stream and everything read afterwards was garbage.

    The three UUIDs are read as four consecutive 32-bit words. Byte order inside a UUID only affects how the value prints - what matters for stream alignment is the width: 129 bits when present, 1 bit when absent.

    FieldTypeNotes
    ServerOwnerGuidGuid?
    WorldUuidGuid?
    ServerUuidGuid?
    ServerOwnerNamestring?
    ChatHoststring?
    MaxUsersuint
    MinUsersRequiredToPlayuint
    ChatPortushort
    GameModeEntityIdint
    IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayersbool
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.ServerInfo);

    WriteUuid(bitStream, ServerOwnerGuid);
    WriteUuid(bitStream, WorldUuid);
    WriteUuid(bitStream, ServerUuid);

    bitStream.WriteString(ServerOwnerName);
    bitStream.WriteString(ChatHost);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(MaxUsers, UserCountMax), UserCountMax);
    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(MinUsersRequiredToPlay, UserCountMax), UserCountMax);

    bitStream.Write(ChatPort);

    bitStream.Write(GameModeEntityId);

    bitStream.Write(IsOpenedToMatchmakingForMorePlayers);

    return bitStream;
}

    Ordinal 75. Reader: FUN_007EBBD0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x4B, ...).

    This one travels both ways - the client has a sender too (FUN_00A51520) - so the same layout serves for receiving it later.

    SourceId    FUN_007EA4B0: 1 presence bit + 32 bits
Shape       FUN_007D6410: ranged, max 0x38 -> 6 bits
Position    FUN_007D66A0: X 17 bits, Y 15 bits biased by -0x3FC0, Z 17 bits
Direction   FUN_007D6590: three components, ranged max 0x80 -> 8 bits each
Radius      FUN_007D6390: ranged, max 0x18 -> 5 bits

    Same position reader as PlayerSpawned and EventEffect, so the same 64-units-per-world-unit scale and the same narrow biased Y. Direction is clamped to +-0x40 by FUN_0077A330 right after it is read, which is where the bias here comes from.

    FieldTypeNotes

    Shapeuint
    PositionVector3
    DirectionVector3Int

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Shape, ShapeMax), ShapeMax);

    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.X);
    WriteVertical(bitStream, Position.Y);
    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.Z);

    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.X);
    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Y);
    WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Z);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Radius, RadiusMax), RadiusMax);

    #76 RPCSetInputMovement C2S handled

    Ordinal76 (0x4C)
    Client senderFUN_00b24140 0x00B24140
    Emulator handlerSetInputMovement.Handle
    Emulator classSetInputMovement Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetInputMovement.cs

    RPCSetInputMovement, ordinal 76, client to server (send stub FUN_00B24140). The movement input of the player's character, sent whenever it changes:

    direction 3 x ranged max 0x80 of (component + 0x40) 3 x 8 bits (FUN_008621B0) speed ranged max 0x78 7 bits (FUN_00B23870)

    Idle is "40 40 40 00" (zero vector, speed 0). The server stores it on ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent, whose inputmovementdirection / inputmovementspeed bindings sync it back to every client, the owner included.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent.DirectionMax, out var x)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent.DirectionMax, out var y)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent.DirectionMax, out var z)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent.SpeedMax, out var speed)) return false;

    if (connection.PlayerEntity.TryGetComponent<ClientCharacterPhysicsComponent>(out var physics))
    {
        physics.InputMovementDirection = (x - 0x40, y - 0x40, z - 0x40);
        physics.InputMovementSpeed = (uint)speed;
    }

    return true;
}

    #77 RPCEntityPerformedAbility both partial

    Ordinal77 (0x4D)
    Client senderFUN_00a77b10 0x00A77B10
    Client receive stubFUN_007f21e0 0x007F21E0
    Emulator classEntityPerformedAbility Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EntityPerformedAbility.cs

    Ordinal 77. Reader: FUN_007F21E0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x4D, ...). The client has a sender too (FUN_00A77B10), so this layout serves both directions.

    EntityId    32 bits, full width -> big-endian
AbilityId   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
(FUN_007DFFD0 reads nothing - it is a constructor priming defaults)
Origin      1 presence bit + 32 bits
Target      1 presence bit + 32 bits
Position    FUN_007D66A0: X 17 bits, Y 15 bits biased by -0x3FC0, Z 17 bits
Direction   FUN_007D6590: three components, ranged max 0x80 -> 8 bits each
Power       FUN_00CC1740: ranged, max 0x20 -> 6 bits, scaled by 0.03125 to 0.0..1.0
Impact      one AbilityTarget (FUN_007E8220)
Charge      1 presence bit + 32 bits
Flag        1 bit
Slot        FUN_007D6D80: ranged, max 8 -> 4 bits

    This was the deepest of the deferred packets - three levels of nesting - and most of that depth turned out to be constructors. FUN_007DFFD0 and FUN_007E0050 read nothing at all; they only prime locals with the same defaults seen elsewhere (0xFFFFC040 for a biased Y, 0xFFFFFFC0 for direction components). Once those are set aside only two real readers remain.

    Power uses the same scale AlterActivePhaseByFactor does - DAT_012C1214 is 0.03125 - so set it as a real number from 0 to 1.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    AbilityIdint
    Originuint?
    Targetuint?
    PositionVector3
    DirectionVector3Int

    Each component is clamped to -64..+64 by the client.

    Powerfloat

    0.0 to 1.0, in steps of a thirty-second.

    ImpactAbilityTarget
    Chargeuint?
    Flagbool
    Slotuint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.EntityPerformedAbility);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)AbilityId);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, Origin);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, Target);

    AbilityTarget.WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.X);
    AbilityTarget.WriteVertical(bitStream, Position.Y);
    AbilityTarget.WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.Z);

    AbilityTarget.WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.X);
    AbilityTarget.WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Y);
    AbilityTarget.WriteDirection(bitStream, Direction.Z);

    var powerUnits = (uint)Math.Clamp((int)(Power / PowerScale), 0, (int)PowerMax);
    bitStream.WriteRanged(powerUnits, PowerMax);

    Impact.Encode(bitStream);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, Charge);

    bitStream.Write(Flag);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Slot, SlotMax), SlotMax);

    return bitStream;
}

    #81 RPCExecuteEntityAction C2S handled

    Ordinal81 (0x51)
    Client senderFUN_00798040 0x00798040
    Emulator handlerExecuteEntityAction.Handle
    Emulator classExecuteEntityAction Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ExecuteEntityAction.cs
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!bitStream.Read(out int srcEntityID))
        return false;

    if (!bitStream.Read(out int targetEntityID))
        return false;

    var actionCrc = 0;
    var hasActionCrc = bitStream.ReadBit();

    if (hasActionCrc && !bitStream.Read(out actionCrc))
        return false;

    _actions.TryGetValue((uint)actionCrc, out var actionName);

    Debug.WriteLine($"srcEntityID: {srcEntityID}, targetEntityID: {targetEntityID}, actionCrc: {actionCrc}, actionName: {actionName}", nameof(ExecuteEntityAction));

    return true;
}

    #89 RPCCoreTemperatureAdjustment S2C implemented

    Ordinal89 (0x59)
    Client receive stubFUN_007eed00 0x007EED00
    Emulator classCoreTemperatureAdjustment Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/CoreTemperatureAdjustment.cs

    Ordinal 89, server to client. Reader: FUN_007EED00 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x59, ...).

    EntityId      32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Temperature   FUN_007D6D00: 8 bits, then the client subtracts 100 and clamps to -100..100

    The width helper here is FUN_00EA7360, which had not turned up before: it is the 8-bit sibling of FUN_00EA7260, returning 8 minus the bits needed for its argument. Called with 0xC8 (200) it returns 0, so the field is a full 8 bits carrying 0..200, and the client reads it as a signed temperature by taking away the 100 bias.

    Set Temperature as the real signed value; the bias and the clamp happen here.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    Temperatureint

    -100 to +100.

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.CoreTemperatureAdjustment);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);

    var raw = (byte)(Math.Clamp(Temperature, TemperatureMin, TemperatureMax) + TemperatureBias);

    bitStream.WriteBits([raw], 8, true);

    return bitStream;
}

    #91 RPCXPGained S2C implemented

    Ordinal91 (0x5B)
    Client receive stubFUN_008021b0 0x008021B0
    Emulator classXPGained Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/XPGained.cs

    Ordinal 91, server to client. Reader: FUN_008021B0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x5B, ...).

    EntityId          32 bits, full width -> big-endian
JobChallenge      FUN_007D7E90: 16-bit index, width from [0x01487BCC] + 0x1D28
Job               FUN_007D7E10: 16-bit index, width from [0x01487BCC] + 0x1D34
Amount            1 presence bit + 32 bits
Item              FUN_007E7F40: 1 bit, and if set an InventoryItemRef plus 32 bits
Reason            FUN_007E8050: 1 bit, and if set a string
Bonus             1 presence bit + 32 bits
Total             32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    The two index fields are the same GeoData-driven mechanism MapSpec's searchable slots use, and the alphabetical descriptor grid identifies them: rank 103 at 0x1D28 is JobChallenges (2010 entries, so 11 bits) and rank 104 at 0x1D34 is Jobs (5 entries, so 3 bits). An XP award tied to a job challenge within a job is exactly what the name says.

    The widths come from lists inside the client, so if a future build changes those counts the widths move with them. Read the dwords at those two addresses from a running client if the packet ever mis-parses.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    JobChallengeuint
    Jobuint
    Amountuint?
    ItemInventoryItemRef?
    ItemCountuint
    Reasonstring?
    Bonusuint?
    Totalint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.XPGained);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);

    bitStream.WriteRangedIndex(JobChallenge, JobChallengeCount);
    bitStream.WriteRangedIndex(Job, JobCount);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, Amount);

    if (Item is null)
    {
        bitStream.Write0();
    }
    else
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        Item.Encode(bitStream);
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, ItemCount);
    }

    if (Reason is null)
    {
        bitStream.Write0();
    }
    else
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        bitStream.WriteString(Reason);
    }

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, Bonus);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)Total);

    return bitStream;
}

    #97 RPCPvPWaitingInfo S2C implemented

    Ordinal97 (0x61)
    Client receive stubFUN_00925950 0x00925950
    Emulator classPvPWaitingInfo Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PvPWaitingInfo.cs

    Ordinal 97, server to client. Reader: FUN_00925950 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x61, ...).

    One 64-bit value, read by FUN_00C715B0 as 0x40 bits with an eight-byte swap - so it goes out big-endian, the 64-bit version of the same rule the 32-bit fields follow. Everything after the read in that function is local formatting, not more reading.

    A single wide number on a "waiting info" packet is almost certainly a deadline or a timestamp, but nothing here names itself.

    WriteUInt64(bitStream, Value);

    return bitStream;

    #105 RPCNarrativeCompleted C2S handled

    Ordinal105 (0x69)
    Client senderFUN_00954cc0 0x00954CC0
    Emulator handlerNarrativeCompletedReceived.Handle
    Emulator classNarrativeCompleted Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/NarrativeCompleted.cs
    Emulator classNarrativeCompletedReceived Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/NarrativeCompletedReceived.cs
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.NarrativeCompleted);

    return bitStream;
}

    Handles ordinal 105 (RPCNarrativeCompleted) arriving FROM the client.

    Observed as 'ef 49 7c c0 00 00 00 10' - a direct (non-escaped) id byte 0xEF, so 239 - 134 = 105. Three arrived back to back, which is why the payload is worth logging before deciding what, if anything, the server owes in reply: two of the three were byte-identical and one differed in a single byte (0x49 vs 0x4a), so there is some per-narrative discriminator in there.

    Nothing is sent back yet. There is no evidence about what a reply should contain, and the packet was being dropped as unhandled before this, so simply seeing it decoded is the point.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    // 105 is below the escape threshold, so its id was a single byte.
    var remainingBits = (uint)Math.Max(0, totalBits - 8);
    var payload = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(payload, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);
    var hex = string.Join(" ", payload.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    Console.WriteLine($"[NarrativeCompleted] Received (ordinal 105) from the client. "
        + $"remainingBitsAfterId={remainingBits} readOk={readOk} payload: {hex}");

    return true;
}

    #107 RPCPlayFullScreenVideo S2C implemented

    Ordinal107 (0x6B)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f9670 0x007F9670
    Emulator classPlayFullScreenVideo Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PlayFullScreenVideo.cs

    Alpha 10 layout, read out of the client.

    Stub: FUN_007F9670 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x6B, ...)

    VideoIndex FUN_007D8310 ranged 16-bit index, width from the client's Videos list Unknown inline 32 bits, full width (so big-endian)

    The video is picked by its position in the client's own Videos list, not by name. The old code sent a CRC32 of the name, so the client took the top two bits of that hash as the index and then tried to read 32 more bits that were not in the packet. Whichever video it started was down to those two bits of the hash.

    Indices are 1-based with 0 meaning "none" - the client's logger special-cases 0 and bounds- checks the rest against the list length + 1. GeoData has three videos, so the count is 4 and the field is 2 bits:

    1  CGIntro_Video1          -> transitions to the Eva_Intro animated sequence
2  CGIntro_Video2          -> transitions to TeleportToWorld
3  GCIntro_StoryTextVideo  -> looping, file SkySaga_IntroStoryBackground_Loop,
                              story text INTRO_STORY_TEXT, transitions to CGIntro_Video1

    The trailing 32-bit field is not identified. The client's logger prints it as a plain number and the name pointer does not survive decompilation. It is the last thing in the packet, so a wrong value cannot shift anything - but it is a guess, and zero is only a placeholder.

    FieldTypeNotes
    CGIntroVideo1const uint
    CGIntroVideo2const uint
    GCIntroStoryTextVideoconst uint
    VideoIndexuint
    Unknownuint

    Purpose unknown - see the note on the class.

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.PlayFullScreenVideo);

    bitStream.WriteRangedIndex(VideoIndex, VideoCount);

    var unknownBytes = BitConverter.GetBytes(Unknown);

    Array.Reverse(unknownBytes);

    bitStream.WriteBits(unknownBytes, 32, true);

    return bitStream;
}

    #108 RPCFullScreenVideoCompleted C2S handled

    Ordinal108 (0x6C)
    Client senderFUN_007e50b0 0x007E50B0
    Emulator handlerFullScreenVideoCompleted.Handle
    Emulator classFullScreenVideoCompleted Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/FullScreenVideoCompleted.cs
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();
    var totalBytes = bitStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed();

    var remainingBits = (uint)(totalBits - 8);
    var buffer = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(buffer, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);

    var hex = string.Join(" ", buffer.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    Console.WriteLine($"[FullScreenVideoCompleted] Received. totalBytes={totalBytes} totalBits={totalBits} "
        + $"remainingBitsAfterId={remainingBits} readOk={readOk} raw: {hex} (was playing: {connection.CurrentVideoFileName})");

    connection.CurrentVideoFileName = null;

    return true;
}

    #111 RPCActionShowDialog S2C implemented

    Ordinal111 (0x6F)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ea750 0x007EA750
    Emulator classActionShowDialog Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/ActionShowDialog.cs

    Ordinal 111, server to client. Reader: FUN_007EA750 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x6F, ...).

    DialogIndex   ranged, max 0x7D -> 7 bits
EntityId      1 presence bit + 32 bits
TargetId      1 presence bit + 32 bits
Flag          1 bit

    This is the packet that puts dialogue and narrative text on screen, and the server has never sent one - the class was a bare stub until now. The client answers with NarrativeCompleted (105) when the player finishes reading, which we already handle and have seen fire.

    One caveat that the layout cannot settle: DialogIndex is a seven-bit index into a list of dialogues held inside the client, and that list is not in GeoData.json - nothing there has a matching shape. So the field is correct on the wire, but which number means which line of text is unknown, and sending an arbitrary index will show an arbitrary dialogue or none at all.

    FieldTypeNotes
    DialogIndexuint

    Index into the client's own dialogue list - see the note on the class.

    EntityIduint?
    TargetIduint?
    Flagbool
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.ActionShowDialog);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(DialogIndex, DialogIndexMax), DialogIndexMax);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, EntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, TargetId);

    bitStream.Write(Flag);

    return bitStream;
}

    #129 RPCBlueprintSourceDataRequested S2C implemented

    Ordinal129 (0x81)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ed6c0 0x007ED6C0
    Emulator classBlueprintSourceDataRequested Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/BlueprintSourceDataRequested.cs

    Ordinal 129, server to client. Reader: FUN_007ED6C0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x81, ...), body read by FUN_007E8380.

    Layers      FUN_007E6D80: count FUN_007D7400, ranged max 0x200 -> 10 bits, escape at 0x200;
            then that many Layer
Props       FUN_007E6AB0: count the same 10-bit shape; then that many Prop
Flag        1 bit

    This was the deepest structure in the protocol - five levels down before it bottomed out - and it is the actual contents of a blueprint, so the depth is earned rather than accidental.

    Every list here uses a count of 10 or 16 bits with the usual escape, and the leaf record (FUN_007E89E0) is three flat fields. The 88-byte prop element opens with the same InventoryItemRef that KillOccurred and the mystery boxes carry, which is what finally made it tractable.

    Names are by role. Nothing in this packet keeps its logger strings.

    FieldTypeNotes
    LayersList<Layer>
    PropsList<Prop>
    Flagbool
    Layerclass

    FUN_007E8AE0 - a byte and a list of 32-bit words (FUN_007E77E0).

    Kindbyte
    ValuesList<uint>
    Propclass

    FUN_007E8890 - an item reference and a list of placements (FUN_007E6CB0).

    ItemInventoryItemRef
    PlacementsList<Placement>
    Placementclass

    FUN_007E89E0 - the leaf. Three flat fields, no nesting below.

    Positionuint
    Rotationbyte

    ranged, max 4 -> 3 bits

    Variantbyte

    ranged, max 7 -> 3 bits

    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.BlueprintSourceDataRequested);

    WriteCount(bitStream, (uint)Layers.Count, SectionCountMax);

    foreach (var layer in Layers)
    {
        bitStream.WriteBits([layer.Kind], 8, true);

        WriteCount(bitStream, (uint)layer.Values.Count, InnerCountMax);

        foreach (var value in layer.Values)
            InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, value);
    }

    WriteCount(bitStream, (uint)Props.Count, SectionCountMax);

    foreach (var prop in Props)
    {
        prop.Item.Encode(bitStream);

        WriteCount(bitStream, (uint)prop.Placements.Count, InnerCountMax);

        foreach (var placement in prop.Placements)
        {
            InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, placement.Position);

            bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(placement.Rotation, RotationMax), RotationMax);
            bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(placement.Variant, VariantMax), VariantMax);
        }
    }

    bitStream.Write(Flag);

    return bitStream;
}

    #140 RPCTransferToServer S2C implemented

    Ordinal140 (0x8C)
    Client receive stubFUN_008016f0 0x008016F0
    Emulator classTransferToServer Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/TransferToServer.cs

    Ordinal 140, server to client. Reader: FUN_008016F0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x8C, ...).

    WorldUuid    1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
ServerUuid   1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
Host         string
Port         32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Token        FUN_007D6E60: a 32-bit big-endian length, then that many bytes

    The last field is a length-prefixed blob, not a string: FUN_007D6E60 reads a 32-bit count, allocates, reads count * 8 bits, and hands the buffer to FUN_007DF4E0. That is a different shape from WriteString, which uses a one-bit small/large flag and an 8-bit length, so it needs its own writer.

    This is the packet that moves a client to another server, which is what a teleport ultimately is. Names are by role - two identities, an address, a port and an opaque credential is the obvious reading, but nothing here names itself.

    FieldTypeNotes
    WorldUuidGuid?
    ServerUuidGuid?
    Hoststring?
    Portint
    Tokenbyte[]
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.TransferToServer);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, WorldUuid);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, ServerUuid);

    bitStream.WriteString(Host);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)Port);

    WriteBlob(bitStream, Token);

    return bitStream;
}

    #151 RPCMessageOfTheDay S2C implemented

    Ordinal151 (0x97)
    Client receive stubFUN_009e5560 0x009E5560
    Emulator classMessageOfTheDay Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/MessageOfTheDay.cs

    Ordinal 151, server to client. Reader: FUN_009E5560 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x97, ...).

    Title     string
(FUN_00CFA920 reads nothing - it calls FUN_00EA7A60, which only zeroes two words)
Lines     FUN_009E53C0: count via FUN_009E4870, ranged max 0x1A -> 5 bits, escape at 26;
          then per entry an 8-bit code (max 0x19, clamped) and a string
Body      string
Footer    string
Link      string
Version   32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    The line codes are clamped to 0..0x19 by the client, and zero is treated specially - the reader writes 0 through unchanged and anything else as-is, so 0 is likely "plain". Names are by role.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Titlestring?
    LinesList<Line>
    Bodystring?
    Footerstring?
    Linkstring?
    Versionuint
    Lineclass
    Kinduint
    Textstring?
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.MessageOfTheDay);

    bitStream.WriteString(Title);

    var count = (uint)Lines.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, LineCountMax), LineCountMax);

    if (count >= LineCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var line in Lines)
    {
        bitStream.WriteBits([(byte)Math.Min(line.Kind, LineKindMax)], 8, true);
        bitStream.WriteString(line.Text);
    }

    bitStream.WriteString(Body);
    bitStream.WriteString(Footer);
    bitStream.WriteString(Link);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Version);

    return bitStream;
}

    #153 RPCEntityAdd S2C implemented

    Ordinal153 (0x99)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f1140 0x007F1140
    Emulator classEntityAdd Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EntityAdd.cs

    Reader: FUN_007F1140 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x99, ...)

    NameHash FUN_007EA4B0 presence bit + 32 bits Id inline 32 bits ParentId FUN_007E7D10 presence bit + 32 bits SyncData FUN_007D7490 escape bit, then 18- or 32-bit length FUN_00EA6640 that many bits

    The field order and all three 32-bit values were already right. What was missing is one bit. FUN_007D7490 reads a flag before the length:

    if (ReadBit()) width = 32 - NumBitsRequired(0xFFFFFFFF);   // 32 bits
else           width = 32 - NumBitsRequired(0x20000);      // 18 bits

    The server wrote the 18-bit length with no flag in front of it. The arithmetic on any packet in the log shows it: the Airship entity came to 219 bits, which is 16 + 1 + 32 + 32 + 1 + 18 + 119 - exactly one bit short of what the client consumes.

    The consequence is not a one-bit rounding error. The client takes the top bit of the length as the flag, then reads 18 bits that are the remaining 17 length bits plus the first bit of the payload, so the length it ends up with is roughly double the real one. For the player entity, 1950 bits of sync data were being read as about 3900 - past the end of the packet. Every EntityAdd was landing as garbage: the NPC, the airship, TimeOfDay, the player and all eighteen inventory items.

    The 32-bit branch is read through FUN_007187F0, the ranged predicate, so that length stays little-endian - unlike the full-width fields above it, which go out big-endian via bitStream.Write.

    FieldTypeNotes
    NameHashuint?
    Idint
    ParentIdint?
    SyncDataBitStream
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(Ordinal);

    bitStream.WriteOptional(NameHash, (value) =>
    {
        bitStream.Write((int)value);
    });

    bitStream.Write(Id);

    bitStream.WriteOptional(ParentId, bitStream.Write);

    var syncDataBits = SyncData.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    WriteSyncDataLength(bitStream, syncDataBits);

    // BitStream.Write(BitStream) reads from SyncData and moves its read pointer, so a second
    // Serialize() wrote the length but no data. SendInitialEntities serializes once for its hex
    // log and again in Send(), so every EntityAdd reached the client as a bare 13-byte header and
    // the client never applied any entity's initial state (TimeOfDay, health, ...). Measured
    // 2026-10-12 in the client: RPC stream 104 bits, sync blob 3 bits. Rewind first.
    if (syncDataBits > 0)
    {
        SyncData.ResetReadPointer();
        bitStream.Write(SyncData, syncDataBits);
    }

    return bitStream;
}

    #154 RPCEntitySync S2C implemented

    Ordinal154 (0x9A)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f2e70 0x007F2E70
    Emulator classEntitySync Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EntitySync.cs

    Ordinal 154, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F2E70 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x9A, ...).

    Id         32 bits, full width -> big-endian
SyncData   FUN_007D7490: escape bit, then an 18- or 32-bit length, then that many bits

    Identical tail to EntityAdd, and it had the identical bug plus one more.

    1. The escape bit before the length was missing. FUN_007D7490 reads a flag first: set means a 32-bit length follows, clear means 18 bits. Without it the client takes the top bit of our 18-bit length as the flag and then reads 18 bits that are the remaining 17 length bits plus the first bit of the payload - so the length it computes is roughly double the real one.

    2. WriteBits was called without alignBitsToRight. Every other call in the project passes true; the two-argument overload leaves the trailing partial byte aligned the other way, so even the bits that were written landed wrong.

    This is the continuous state stream - it goes out for every changed entity, every tick - so unlike EntityAdd, which only fires on spawn, this one was corrupting gameplay traffic constantly.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Idint
    SyncDataBitStream
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(Ordinal);

    bitStream.Write(Id);

    var syncDataBits = SyncData.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    WriteSyncDataLength(bitStream, syncDataBits);

    // BitStream.Write(BitStream) reads from SyncData and moves its read pointer, so a second
    // Serialize() wrote the length but no data. SendInitialEntities serializes once for its hex
    // log and again in Send(), so every EntityAdd reached the client as a bare 13-byte header and
    // the client never applied any entity's initial state (TimeOfDay, health, ...). Measured
    // 2026-10-12 in the client: RPC stream 104 bits, sync blob 3 bits. Rewind first.
    if (syncDataBits > 0)
    {
        SyncData.ResetReadPointer();
        bitStream.Write(SyncData, syncDataBits);
    }

    return bitStream;
}

    #155 RPCEntityMoved C2S handled

    Ordinal155 (0x9B)
    Client senderFUN_00b2a460 0x00B2A460
    Emulator handlerEntityMoved.Handle
    Emulator classEntityMoved Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/EntityMoved.cs

    RPCEntityMoved, ordinal 155, client to server (send stub FUN_00B2A460). The owning client reports its character's position, 64 bits, no entity id:

    X ranged max 0x10000, 1/64 voxel 17 bits (FUN_00859080) Y ranged max 0x7F80 of (y + 0x3FC0), 1/64 voxel 15 bits Z ranged max 0x10000, 1/64 voxel 17 bits yaw ranged max 0x6400 of (yaw + 0x3200), 1/32 deg 15 bits (FUN_00859000)

    This used to read a 32-bit entity id first and treated every Y as unsigned, so it failed on every packet and the server never saw the player move.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(0x10000, out var x)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(0x7F80, out var y)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(0x10000, out var z)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(0x6400, out var yaw)) return false;

    if (connection.PlayerEntity.TryGetComponent<SmoothedTransformComponent>(out var smoothedTransformComponent))
    {
        smoothedTransformComponent.Position = new Vector3(x / 64f, (y - 0x3FC0) / 64f, z / 64f);
        smoothedTransformComponent.YawDegrees = (yaw - 0x3200) * 0.03125f;
    }

    return true;
}

    #159 RPCPlayerSpawned S2C implemented

    Ordinal159 (0x9F)
    Client receive stubFUN_007fac30 0x007FAC30
    Emulator classPlayerSpawned Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PlayerSpawned.cs

    Alpha 10 layout, read out of the client.

    Stub: FUN_007FAC30 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x9F, ...) Position: FUN_007D66A0 Angles: FUN_007D6510

    The packet used to go out empty - id only, no body. The client reads four fields from it:

    EntityId    32 bits
Position    X 17 bits, Y 15 bits biased by -0x3FC0, Z 17 bits
Yaw         15 bits, biased by -0x3200, clamped to +-0x3200
Pitch       15 bits, same encoding as Yaw

    With an empty body those reads fall through and the client keeps the defaults it primed the locals with: position (0, -0x3FC0, 0) and both angles at -0x3200. So the player was being spawned at a default point facing a default direction, regardless of anything the server thought it had placed.

    Note the Y component. Entity sync (TransformComponent.TrySync) writes all three position components as 17 bits with no bias; this packet does not. Y here is 15 bits over 0..0x7F80 with -0x3FC0 applied on read, which is +-16320 units, or +-255 world units at the 64 units-per-world-unit scale the rest of the project uses. Do not copy the sync encoding here.

    Field names could not be recovered - the logger's name pointers do not survive decompilation in this stub - so these are named by role. The order and the widths come from the reader and are solid.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    PositionVector3
    YawDegreesfloat
    PitchDegreesfloat
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.PlayerSpawned);

    bitStream.Write(EntityId);

    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.X);
    WriteVertical(bitStream, Position.Y);
    WriteHorizontal(bitStream, Position.Z);

    WriteAngle(bitStream, YawDegrees);
    WriteAngle(bitStream, PitchDegrees);

    return bitStream;
}

    #160 RPCSetLookAtDirection C2S handled

    Ordinal160 (0xA0)
    Client senderFUN_0086d540 0x0086D540
    Emulator handlerSetLookAtDirection.Handle
    Emulator classSetLookAtDirection Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SetLookAtDirection.cs

    RPCSetLookAtDirection, ordinal 160, client to server (send stub FUN_0086D540): pitch then yaw, each written by FUN_00859000 as ranged max 0x6400 of (value + 0x3200), 15 bits, in 1/32 degree. There is no look-at mode in front of them (the old handler read 4 bits of one, so both angles were misread).

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(AngleMax, out var rawPitch)) return false;
    if (!bitStream.ReadInt32(AngleMax, out var rawYaw)) return false;

    connection.LookPitch = (rawPitch - AngleBias) / 32f;
    connection.LookYaw = (rawYaw - AngleBias) / 32f;

    return true;
}

    #162 RPCSaveCharacterName C2S handled

    Ordinal162 (0xA2)
    Client senderFUN_008113b0 0x008113B0
    Emulator handlerSaveCharacterName.Handle
    Emulator classSaveCharacterName Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SaveCharacterName.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 1× (C2S)

    FieldJSON type
    namestring
    Example payload
    {
  "name": "MiNo"
}

    RPCSaveCharacterName, ordinal 162, client to server only (send stub FUN_008113B0). Payload: the name as a string ({"name":"MiNo"} in the client's RPC log).

    GameState_CharacterCreation (game state 0xE) sends it from FUN_00810C00 once /api/account/name/checkName has answered "valid", then waits (state +0x84 = 2) for CharacterCreationResponse. That reply is routed by FUN_007150E0 to FUN_00810710 only while the game state is 0xE: result 0 continues, result 1 logs "Character Saved Unsuccessful".

    The Player must also get IsCharacterCustomised = 1. Otherwise faGame_ShowCharacterCustomisation reopens the creator straight away and the client loops Gameplay / CharacterCreation.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    // The ordinal is escaped (0xFF + 41); the dispatcher has already consumed it.
    var name = bitStream.ReadString();

    Console.WriteLine($"[CharacterCreation] SaveCharacterName: '{name}'.");

    var player = connection.PlayerEntity;

    if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(name) && player.TryGetComponent<ClientPlayernameComponent>(out var playername))
        playername.Playername = name;

    if (player.TryGetComponent<ClientCharacterCustomisationComponent>(out var customisation))
    {
        if (connection.CustomisationPayload is { } data)
            customisation.CustomisationData = data;

        customisation.IsCharacterCustomised = true;
    }

    connection.Send(new CharacterCreationResponse { Result = 0 });
    connection.SaveState();

    Console.WriteLine("[CharacterCreation] Character saved, CharacterCreationResponse(0) sent.");

    return true;
}

    #163 RPCCharacterCreationResponse S2C implemented

    Ordinal163 (0xA3)
    Client receive stubFUN_007ede70 0x007EDE70
    Emulator classCharacterCreationResponse Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/CharacterCreationResponse.cs

    Fields as the client logs them seen 1× (S2C)

    FieldJSON type
    responseint
    Example payload
    {
  "response": 0
}

    Ordinal 163, server to client. Reader: FUN_007EDE70 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xA3, ...).

    One ranged field, maximum 4, so 3 bits. That is the whole packet - a small result code, which is what a response to character creation would be.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Resultuint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.CharacterCreationResponse);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Result, ResultMax), ResultMax);

    return bitStream;
}

    #166 RPCMysteryBoxPrizesDetermined S2C implemented

    Ordinal166 (0xA6)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f7b40 0x007F7B40
    Emulator classMysteryBoxPrizesDetermined Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/MysteryBoxPrizesDetermined.cs

    Ordinal 166, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F7B40 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xA6, ...).

    BoxId     32 bits, full width -> big-endian
Prizes    FUN_007E7320: count ranged max 8 (escape at 8), then that many MysteryBoxPrize
Seed      FUN_007D6830: ranged, max 0x3C00 -> 14 bits
Unnamed   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
    FieldTypeNotes
    BoxIdint
    PrizesList<MysteryBoxPrize>
    Seeduint
    Unnamedint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.MysteryBoxPrizesDetermined);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)BoxId);

    var count = (uint)Prizes.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, PrizeCountMax), PrizeCountMax);

    if (count >= PrizeCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var prize in Prizes)
        prize.Encode(bitStream);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Seed, SeedMax), SeedMax);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)Unnamed);

    return bitStream;
}

    Ordinal168 (0xA8)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f7630 0x007F7630
    Emulator classMysteryBoxOpenedNotification Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/MysteryBoxOpenedNotification.cs

    Ordinal 168, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F7630 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xA8, ...).

    EntityId     32 bits, full width -> big-endian
BoxId        32 bits, full width -> big-endian
PlayerName   string
Prize        one MysteryBoxPrize (FUN_007E92D0)
    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    BoxIdint
    PlayerNamestring?
    PrizeMysteryBoxPrize
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)BoxId);

    bitStream.WriteString(PlayerName);

    Prize.Encode(bitStream);

    return bitStream;
}

    #234 RPCRunMeandrosActions S2C implemented

    Ordinal234 (0xEA)
    Client receive stubFUN_007fd110 0x007FD110
    Emulator classRunMeandrosActions Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/RunMeandrosActions.cs

    Ordinal 234, server to client. Reader: FUN_007FD110 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0xEA, ...).

    ActionListId   1 presence bit + 32 bits
EntityId       32 bits, full width -> big-endian

    Field names are by role; the widths and the order come from the reader.

    FieldTypeNotes
    ActionListIduint?
    EntityIdint
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.RunMeandrosActions);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, ActionListId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);

    return bitStream;
}

    #241 RPCDebugRenderInfoAllAIEntities C2S handled

    Ordinal241 (0xF1)
    Emulator handlerPerformVoxelActions.Handle
    Emulator classDebugRenderInfoAllAIEntities Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/DebugRenderInfoAllAIEntities.cs
    Emulator classPerformVoxelActions Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PerformVoxelActions.cs
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.DebugRenderInfoAllAIEntities);

    return bitStream;
}
    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{

    if (!ReadLocation(bitStream, out var location)) return false;

    if (!ReadChunkCoordinate(bitStream, out var chunkCoordX)) return false;
    if (!ReadChunkCoordinate(bitStream, out var chunkCoordY)) return false;
    if (!ReadChunkCoordinate(bitStream, out var chunkCoordZ)) return false;

    if (!ReadVoxelCoordinate(bitStream, out var voxelCoordX)) return false;
    if (!ReadVoxelCoordinate(bitStream, out var voxelCoordY)) return false;
    if (!ReadVoxelCoordinate(bitStream, out var voxelCoordZ)) return false;

    if (!ReadSide(bitStream, out var side)) return false;

    if (!ReadPower(bitStream, out var power)) return false;

    if (!ReadPosition(bitStream, out var positionX)) return false;
    if (!ReadPosition(bitStream, out var positionY)) return false;
    if (!ReadPosition(bitStream, out var positionZ)) return false;

    if (!ReadDirection(bitStream, out var directionX)) return false;
    if (!ReadDirection(bitStream, out var directionY)) return false;
    if (!ReadDirection(bitStream, out var directionZ)) return false;

    Debug.WriteLine($"location: {location}, chunkCoord: ({chunkCoordX}, {chunkCoordY}, {chunkCoordZ}), voxelCoord: ({voxelCoordX}, {voxelCoordY}, {voxelCoordZ}), side: {side}, power: {power}, position: ({positionX}, {positionY}, {positionZ}), direction: ({directionX}, {directionY}, {directionZ})", nameof(PerformVoxelActions));

    var world = connection.WorldManager;
    var chunkPos = new Vector3Int(chunkCoordX, chunkCoordY, chunkCoordZ);
    var voxelPos = new Vector3Int(voxelCoordX, voxelCoordY, voxelCoordZ);

    int blockId = 0;
    if (location == ActionLocation.LeftHand && connection.LeftHandBlockId.HasValue)
    {
        voxelPos += new Vector3Int(directionX, directionY, directionZ);
        blockId = connection.LeftHandBlockId.Value;
    }
    if (location == ActionLocation.RightHand && connection.RightHandBlockId.HasValue)
    {
        voxelPos += new Vector3Int(directionX, directionY, directionZ);
        blockId = connection.RightHandBlockId.Value;
    }

    world.ChunkManager.SetVoxel(chunkPos, voxelPos, (byte)blockId);

    Debug.WriteLine($"Voxel at chunk {chunkPos}, voxel {voxelPos}, blockType {blockId}", nameof(PerformVoxelActions));

    return true;
}

    #243 RPCDebugSetShortResetProgressDeadlines S2C implemented

    Ordinal243 (0xF3)
    Emulator classDebugSetShortResetProgressDeadlines Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/DebugSetShortResetProgressDeadlines.cs
    Emulator classSendChatChannelData Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SendChatChannelData.cs
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.DebugSetShortResetProgressDeadlines);

    return bitStream;
}
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(Ordinal);

    bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(8), 32 - Util.NumBitsRequiredUInt32(8));
    bitStream.Write1();
    bitStream.Write(1);

    bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(1), 32 - Util.NumBitsRequiredUInt32(8));
    bitStream.WriteString("WorldChat");
    bitStream.Write1();

    bitStream.WriteString("System");
    bitStream.WriteString("Global");

    return bitStream;
}

    #267 RPCPhotoValidated S2C implemented

    Ordinal267 (0x10B)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f8e80 0x007F8E80
    Emulator classPhotoValidated Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/PhotoValidated.cs

    Ordinal 267, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F8E80 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x10B, ...).

    Result     FUN_007D7630: an escape bit, then 7 bits, or 32 if the bit is set
PhotoUuid  1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
OwnerUuid  1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
    FieldTypeNotes
    Resultuint
    PhotoUuidGuid?
    OwnerUuidGuid?
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.PhotoValidated);

    if (Result <= ResultShortMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write0();
        bitStream.WriteRanged(Result, ResultShortMax);
    }
    else
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Result);
    }

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, PhotoUuid);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, OwnerUuid);

    return bitStream;
}

    #268 RPCRequestStartAnimationSequence C2S handled

    Ordinal268 (0x10C)
    Client senderFUN_009615c0 0x009615C0
    Emulator handlerRequestStartAnimationSequenceReceived.Handle
    Emulator classRequestStartAnimationSequence Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/RequestStartAnimationSequence.cs
    Emulator classRequestStartAnimationSequenceReceived Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/RequestStartAnimationSequenceReceived.cs
    FieldTypeNotes
    SequenceNamestring
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();

    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.RequestStartAnimationSequence);

    bitStream.WriteString(SequenceName);

    return bitStream;
}

    Handles ordinal 268 (RPCRequestStartAnimationSequence) from the client.

    What two captures now confirm:

    EntityId=49  ->  payload 00 00 00 31 38
EntityId=4   ->  payload 00 00 00 04 38

    40 bits: a 32-bit entity id (big-endian on the wire) followed by one byte, 0x38, which did not change between runs. There is no string anywhere in it. That matters, because every class in this family (AnimationSequenceStarting, SetAnimationSequenceProgressData, AnimationSequenceCompleted) was written around a SequenceName string - those shapes are unverified guesses, and the first reply built from them was almost certainly unparseable to the client, which fits the client going silent right after receiving it.

    So this version stops guessing field layouts and echoes the client's own payload bits back. That also sidesteps byte order: the captured id reads big-endian while other writers in this project reverse their bytes by hand, and an echo cannot get that wrong.

    Directions are now settled by observation, and my earlier guess was wrong in two places:

    client -> 268 RequestStartAnimationSequence   (entity id + sequence index)
server -> 272 AnimationSequenceStarting       (echo - this is what started the voice-over)
server -> 269 AnimationSequenceFadeComplete   <-- server to client, never sent until now
client -> 271 AnimationSequenceCompleted      (observed arriving as 'ff 96 ...')
client -> 105 NarrativeCompleted              (observed, three in a row)

    269 is not something the client reports - it never sends one. It is the server telling the client the fade is done, which is exactly the stuck step. The first attempt did send it, but as an empty packet, and this family is keyed by entity id, so the client had nothing to match it against and dropped it. It now goes out echoing the same payload as 272.

    The trailing byte is a sequence index in the top 5 bits: 0x38, 0x40, 0x48 across consecutive steps is 7, 8, 9 shifted left by 3.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();
    var totalBytes = bitStream.GetNumberOfBytesUsed();

    // The id occupied two bytes on the wire (0xFF escape + one byte).
    var remainingBits = (uint)Math.Max(0, totalBits - 16);
    var payload = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(payload, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);
    var hex = string.Join(" ", payload.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    var entityId = payload.Length >= 4
        ? (payload[0] << 24) | (payload[1] << 16) | (payload[2] << 8) | payload[3]
        : -1;
    var trailer = payload.Length >= 5 ? payload[4] : (byte)0;

    var expectedId = connection.PlayerEntity.Id;
    var idNote = entityId == expectedId
        ? "matches the player entity"
        : $"does NOT match the player entity ({expectedId})";

    Console.WriteLine($"[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Received (ordinal 268). "
        + $"totalBytes={totalBytes} totalBits={totalBits} remainingBitsAfterId={remainingBits} "
        + $"readOk={readOk} payload: {hex}");
    Console.WriteLine($"[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Parsed EntityId={entityId} ({idNote}), "
        + $"trailer=0x{trailer:x2} (sequence index {trailer >> 3}).");

    connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
    {
        PacketId = PacketId.AnimationSequenceStarting,
        Payload = payload,
        PayloadBits = remainingBits
    });

    Console.WriteLine($"[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Sent AnimationSequenceStarting (ordinal 272), "
        + $"echoing {remainingBits} payload bits.");

    if (SendFadeComplete)
    {
        connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.AnimationSequenceFadeComplete,
            Payload = payload,
            PayloadBits = remainingBits
        });

        Console.WriteLine("[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Sent AnimationSequenceFadeComplete (ordinal 269), "
            + "echoing the same payload.");
    }

    if (SendProgressData)
    {
        connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.SetAnimationSequenceProgressData,
            Payload = payload,
            PayloadBits = remainingBits
        });

        Console.WriteLine("[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Sent SetAnimationSequenceProgressData (ordinal 270).");
    }

    if (SendCompleted)
    {
        connection.Send(new RawPayloadPacket
        {
            PacketId = PacketId.AnimationSequenceCompleted,
            Payload = payload,
            PayloadBits = remainingBits
        });

        Console.WriteLine("[RequestStartAnimationSequence] Sent AnimationSequenceCompleted (ordinal 271), "
            + "echoing the same payload.");
    }

    return true;
}

    #271 RPCAnimationSequenceCompleted C2S handled

    Handles ordinal 271 (RPCAnimationSequenceCompleted) arriving FROM the client.

    Observed on the wire as 'ff 96 00 00 00 01 48' - escape byte 0x96 plus the 121 bias gives 271, and the payload is the same (entity id + sequence index) pair the client used to request the sequence. So 271 is the client reporting that a sequence it started has finished, not a server command as first assumed.

    Until this handler existed it was logged as an unhandled packet and dropped.

    Handle (client → server parser)
    public static bool Handle(PlayerConnection connection, BitStream bitStream)
{
    var totalBits = bitStream.GetNumberOfBitsUsed();

    // 271 is above the escape threshold, so the id took two bytes.
    var remainingBits = (uint)Math.Max(0, totalBits - 16);
    var payload = new byte[(remainingBits + 7) / 8];

    var readOk = remainingBits == 0 || bitStream.ReadBits(payload, remainingBits, alignBitsToRight: true);
    var hex = string.Join(" ", payload.Select(b => b.ToString("x2")));

    var entityId = payload.Length >= 4
        ? (payload[0] << 24) | (payload[1] << 16) | (payload[2] << 8) | payload[3]
        : -1;
    var trailer = payload.Length >= 5 ? payload[4] : (byte)0;

    Console.WriteLine($"[AnimationSequenceCompleted] Received (ordinal 271) from the client. "
        + $"EntityId={entityId} trailer=0x{trailer:x2} (sequence index {trailer >> 3}) "
        + $"readOk={readOk} payload: {hex}");

    return true;
}

    #272 RPCAnimationSequenceStarting S2C implemented

    Ordinal272 (0x110)
    Client receive stubFUN_007eb7e0 0x007EB7E0
    Emulator classAnimationSequenceStarting Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/AnimationSequenceStarting.cs

    Ordinal 272, server to client. Reader: FUN_007EB7E0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x110, ...).

    EntityId        32 bits, full width -> big-endian
SequenceIndex   FUN_007D79F0: 16 bits, then FUN_007DF5E0 looks the sequence up by it

    48 bits of body. THE SERVER HAS BEEN SENDING 40.

    Until now this went out as a raw echo of the client's ordinal-268 payload, which is five bytes: a 32-bit entity id and one trailing byte (0x38, the sequence index in its top five bits). That is 268's encoding, not 272's. The client reads a 16-bit index here, so it consumed the 0x38 plus eight bits that were not in the packet.

    Reading past the end is exactly the failure mode behind the STATUS_FLOAT_MULTIPLE_TRAPS on ordinal 271 - the same family of packets, the same habit of echoing a payload shaped for a different ordinal. Send this typed instead.

    SequenceIndex is the plain index here (7 for Eva_Leads_Player), not the shifted byte 268 uses. That follows from the reader handing the value straight to a lookup, but it is the one part of this that the layout alone does not prove.

    FieldTypeNotes
    EntityIdint
    SequenceIndexushort
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.AnimationSequenceStarting);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)EntityId);
    bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes(SequenceIndex), 16, true);

    return bitStream;
}

    #287 RPCBatch both partial

    Ordinal287 (0x11F)
    Client senderFUN_00dbd8f0 0x00DBD8F0
    Client receive stubFUN_00dbe1b0 0x00DBE1B0
    Emulator classBatch Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/Batch.cs

    Ordinal 287, server to client. Reader: FUN_00DBE1B0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x11F, ...).

    Payload    FUN_007D6E60: a 32-bit big-endian length, then that many bytes
Channel    FUN_00DBCD90: ranged, max 7 -> 3 bits
Sequence   FUN_00DBCE20: 1 escape bit, then 16 bits, or 32 if the bit is set
Flag       1 bit

    The stub lives among the network plumbing (0x00DBxxxx) rather than with the ordinary packet readers, which is why it looked like a container rather than a packet. It is a packet: the batched contents ride in Payload as an opaque blob, using the same length-prefixed shape TransferToServer uses for its token, and the three fields after it are ordinary.

    Nothing here says how the blob is framed internally - whether it is a run of complete RPCs or something else. That is a separate question from the wire layout, which is settled.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Payloadbyte[]
    Channeluint
    Sequenceuint
    Flagbool
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.Batch);

    TransferToServer.WriteBlob(bitStream, Payload);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Channel, ChannelMax), ChannelMax);

    if (Sequence <= SequenceShortMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write0();
        bitStream.WriteBits(BitConverter.GetBytes((ushort)Sequence), 16, true);
    }
    else
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, Sequence);
    }

    bitStream.Write(Flag);

    return bitStream;
}

    #328 RPCGameQueueInviteFailed S2C implemented

    Ordinal328 (0x148)
    Client receive stubFUN_007f4600 0x007F4600
    Emulator classGameQueueInviteFailed Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/GameQueueInviteFailed.cs

    Ordinal 328, server to client. Reader: FUN_007F4600 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x148, ...).

    The whole body is the structure FUN_007E9570 reads:

    QueueId      1 presence bit + 32 bits
InviterUuid  1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
InviteeUuid  1 presence bit + 4 x 32 bits
FlagA        1 bit
FlagB        1 bit
Timestamp    FUN_00C715B0: 64 bits -> big-endian
FlagC        1 bit
Reason       ranged, max 7 -> 3 bits
FlagD        1 bit

    The four single bits are packed into one byte in the client's structure, at bit positions 0 through 3 in the order read - they are separate fields on the wire, not a bitfield.

    FieldTypeNotes
    QueueIduint?
    InviterUuidGuid?
    InviteeUuidGuid?
    FlagAbool
    FlagBbool
    Timestampulong
    FlagCbool
    Reasonuint
    FlagDbool
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.GameQueueInviteFailed);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUInt32(bitStream, QueueId);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, InviterUuid);
    InventoryItemRef.WriteOptionalUuid(bitStream, InviteeUuid);

    bitStream.Write(FlagA);
    bitStream.Write(FlagB);

    PvPWaitingInfo.WriteUInt64(bitStream, Timestamp);

    bitStream.Write(FlagC);

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(Reason, ReasonMax), ReasonMax);

    bitStream.Write(FlagD);

    return bitStream;
}

    #333 RPCSyncLogicNetwork S2C implemented

    Ordinal333 (0x14D)
    Client receive stubFUN_00800af0 0x00800AF0
    Emulator classSyncLogicNetwork Servers/SkySaga.Game/Packets/SyncLogicNetwork.cs

    Ordinal 333, server to client. Reader: FUN_00800AF0 -> FUN_00BB3E50(1, 0x14D, ...).

    Timestamp   FUN_00C715B0: 64 bits -> big-endian
NetworkId   32 bits, full width -> big-endian
States      FUN_007E7B20: count ranged max 8 (escape at 8), then that many 64-bit values

    The state list is the only place so far where a list carries 64-bit elements - FUN_007E7B20 reads each with the same 0x40-bit, eight-byte-swap pattern PvPWaitingInfo uses for its single value.

    FieldTypeNotes
    Timestampulong
    NetworkIdint
    StatesList<ulong>
    Serialize (server → client wire format)
    public BitStream Serialize()
{
    var bitStream = new BitStream();
    bitStream.WritePacketId(PacketId.SyncLogicNetwork);

    PvPWaitingInfo.WriteUInt64(bitStream, Timestamp);

    InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, (uint)NetworkId);

    var count = (uint)States.Count;

    bitStream.WriteRanged(Math.Min(count, StateCountMax), StateCountMax);

    if (count >= StateCountMax)
    {
        bitStream.Write1();
        InventoryItemRef.WriteUInt32(bitStream, count);
    }

    foreach (var state in States)
        PvPWaitingInfo.WriteUInt64(bitStream, state);

    return bitStream;
}
